# BLOCKERS.md — ช่องทาง escalate ความขัดแย้ง design/spec

> **ไฟล์นี้คือช่องทางเดียว (single channel) สำหรับความขัดแย้ง design/spec ทุกกรณี** — ตาม PLAN.md §0 กฎข้อ 4 และ §10
> เจอความขัดแย้งที่ไม่อยู่ในตารางคำตัดสิน (PLAN.md ภาคผนวก C) → เขียน blocker ที่นี่ แล้วข้ามไป task อื่น **ห้ามเดา ห้ามเลือกเอง**
> Sacred files (PLAN.md §10: OpenAPI · merged migrations · CLAUDE.md ทุกใบ · CI config · secrets · `docs/extract/*` · i18n-full.json) แก้ได้ผ่าน blocker ที่ Wei อนุมัติแล้วเท่านั้น
> ข้อความ UI ที่ไม่มี key ใน `i18n-full.json` = เข้า blocker (PLAN.md §0 กฎข้อ 2)

## วิธีใช้

1. id รูปแบบ `B-xxx` รันต่อเนื่อง · ระบุ task ที่ชน · ตั้งคำถามให้ชัด · เสนอตัวเลือกให้ Wei เลือก
2. ระหว่างรอคำตอบ → เปลี่ยน task ใน `TASKS.md` เป็น `blocked` แล้วหยิบ task อื่น (ยกเว้น blocker ประเภทแจ้งเพื่อทราบ/ยืนยันย้อนหลัง สถานะ "รอ Wei ตอบ — ไม่ block งาน")
3. Wei ตอบแล้ว → บันทึกลงคอลัมน์ "คำตอบ Wei" → นำไปใช้ → เปลี่ยนสถานะเป็น `ตอบแล้ว — นำไปใช้` และปลด task จาก `blocked`
4. ค่าสถานะ: `รอ Wei ตอบ` / `รอ Wei ตอบ — ไม่ block งาน` / `ตอบแล้ว — นำไปใช้` / `ปิด`

persisted (P0-BE-10)" · report-derived 13 datasets skipped · exit 0) · **แก้ในเขตแล้ว (commit WIP บน feature/devops):** host env leak — เดิม `DATABASE_URL: ${DATABASE_URL:-…@postgres:5432…}` / `REDIS_URL: ${REDIS_URL:-…}` ปล่อยให้ host ที่ export `DATABASE_URL=…@127.0.0.1:5433/juneflow` (loop-runner host map pg→5433) **override** เป้าหมายในเน็ตเวิร์ก → migrate-seed `ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:5433` (ในคอนเทนเนอร์ไม่มี 5433) · fix = derive URL จาก `POSTGRES_*` (แหล่งเดียวกับ service postgres) + pin `postgres:5432`/`redis:6379` ตายตัว ไม่ fall-through ไป `${DATABASE_URL}` เปล่า (password ยังมาจาก `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` = กฎ secrets คงอยู่). **บั๊กที่เหลือ (นอกเขต devops → แก้เองไม่ได้):** api container (`node dist/index.js`) crash ตอน boot: `ERR_UNSUPPORTED_NODE_MODULES_TYPE_STRIPPING` ที่ `@juneflow/db/src/client.ts`. **root cause:** `packages/db/package.json` `exports` ชี้ **ซอร์ส TypeScript ดิบ** (`./src/schema/index.ts` · `./src/client.ts`) ไม่มี build/`dist` — ใช้ได้กับ api dev (`tsx watch`) + tests แต่ api **production image** รัน `node dist/index.js` และ `pnpm --filter @juneflow/api deploy --prod /out` แพ็ก `@juneflow/db` เป็น `.ts` ดิบใต้ node_modules → Node 22 ปฏิเสธ strip types ใต้ node_modules → **api + worker crash (exit 1)**. fix อยู่ใน **เขต backend** (`packages/db` และ/หรือ `apps/api/Dockerfile` = `zonePaths.backend`) devops แตะไม่ได้ (PLAN §8). gate "compose up + api healthy" ผ่านไม่ได้จนกว่าปิดปมนี้ | (ก) **[แนะนำ]** เขต backend: ให้ `packages/db` มี build จริง (tsc → `dist`) + ชี้ | B-036 | P1-WEB-01 (port จอ Login · email field label) — **block P1-WEB-01** | จอ Login มี Field label "อีเมล" (extra-screens.jsx:36). B-035 จัด label นี้ไว้ว่า "มี key แล้ว" ผ่าน **phrases channel** (`phrases/"อีเมล"`) จึง**ไม่ได้เพิ่ม dict key `login.email`** (มีแต่ `login.password`). แต่ค้นแล้ว **ไม่มี dict key ใดให้ค่า "อีเมล"** — มีเฉพาะใน `phrases` (และ `nav_i18n`) ซึ่งเป็น channel แบบ **Thai-as-key** (ตัวข้อความไทยคือตัว key เอง เช่น `tp("อีเมล")`, `tn("ไทยเมนู")`). ปัญหา: hook `.claude/hooks/i18n-guard.sh` **บล็อกตัวอักษรไทย (U+0E00–U+0E7F) ทุกตัวใน `apps/web/src/**/*.ts(x)` (ยกเว้น test)** ด้วย exit 2 — ดังนั้น `tp()/tn()` ที่ต้องใส่ literal ไทยของ key **เขียนใน web code ไม่ได้เลย** (phrases/nav channel ใช้ในฝั่ง web ไม่ได้ภายใต้ hook นี้). สรุป: email label ไม่มี key ที่ web ใช้ได้ · web ใช้ได้เฉพาะ **dict key (ASCII)** เท่านั้น · ตาม PLAN §0 กฎ 2 + zone CLAUDE.md — string ไม่มี key ใช้ได้ = ห้ามเดา ห้าม hardcode ไทย ห้าม bypass hook → escalate. อีก 19/20 string map เข้า dict key ได้ครบ (login.* + app.name + common.lang) เหลือ label เดียวนี้ที่ block. **หมายเหตุกว้าง:** channel `tn`(nav) / `tp`(phrases) ทั้งชุดใช้ใน web UI code ไม่ได้ภายใต้ i18n-guard — flag ให้ platform/devops ทราบ | (ก) **[แนะนำ]** Wei เพิ่ม **dict key `login.email` = "อีเมล"** (th/zh/en/ar = ไทย verbatim ตามแนว B-035 · สมมาตรกับ `login.password`) ลง `i18n-full.json` — web ใช้ `t("login.email")` · unblock ทันที trivial · (ข) Wei/platform ปรับ `i18n-guard.sh` ให้อนุญาต literal ไทยเฉพาะใน `tp(...)/tn(...)` (allowlist) เพื่อให้ phrases/nav channel ใช้ใน web ได้ตามที่ B-035 วางไว้ (แก้ hook = devops/platform zone) · (ค) directive อื่นจาก Wei | (ก) P1-WEB-01 login ship แล้ว = resolved · dict key `login.email` = "อีเมล" มีอยู่แล้วทั้ง 2 สำเนา (B-036 = no-op · formalize provenance) (Wei 13 ก.ค.) | ตอบแล้ว — นำไปใช้ (login ship แล้ว · login.email มีแล้ว) |
| B-038 | P1-BE-01 (gate 4.5 rework) — **BETTER_AUTH_SECRET provisioning บน compose dev (cross-zone backend→devops)** | gate 4.5 ตัดสินว่า code-level fallback secret ที่ทำงานเงียบใน production ขัด infra/CLAUDE.md ("no secrets in repo / everything via env") → backend rework แล้ว: api **fail fast ตอน boot** เมื่อ `NODE_ENV=production` + ไม่มี `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET` (dev fallback เหลือเฉพาะ non-production + boot warning · พิสูจน์แล้ว: prod boot ไม่มี secret → exit 1 พร้อม error ชัด · prod + secret → boot ปกติ) — ผลข้างเคียงที่ตั้งใจ: **compose dev (api image = NODE_ENV=production ใน Dockerfile) จะ boot ไม่ได้จนกว่า provision secret** และ `infra/docker-compose.yml` อยู่เขต devops (backend แก้ข้ามเขตไม่ได้) — ให้ provision อย่างไร | (ก) **[แนะนำ · convention เดียวกับ POSTGRES_PASSWORD dev default]** devops เพิ่ม `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET: ${BETTER_AUTH_SECRET:-juneflow-dev-secret}` ใน services `api` + `worker` ของ `infra/docker-compose.yml` (host env / env_file ของ infra override ได้) · ฝั่ง code คง fail-fast ใน production ตามที่ rework แล้ว · (ข) Wei กำหนดวิธี provisioning เอง (เช่น บังคับผ่าน env file ของ infra เท่านั้น — compose ไม่ boot จนกว่าสร้างไฟล์) · (ค) Wei อนุมัติ code-level fallback แบบ commit รอบแรก (ยอมรับ silent prod risk — reviewer ไม่แนะนำ) | — | ✅ Wei ตัดสิน (ก) 2026-07-13 — devops เพิ่ม `BETTER_AUTH_SECRET: ${BETTER_AUTH_SECRET:-juneflow-dev-secret}` ใน api+worker (convention เดียวกับ POSTGRES_PASSWORD) · code คง fail-fast ใน prod · task P0-DEV-06 (ready) · G2-live re-verify หลัง compose |

| B-167 | 🔴 P3 sales.down concurrent double-post — SEQ MODEL CAN'T DEDUP (money · design/spec · b163 live-proven · BLOCKS P3 promote) | The B-165 seq-unique fix (0043) is category-mismatched: booking dedups because a unit is booked ONCE (natural key `booking:<unitId>`), but a unit legitimately has MANY down instalments → **no natural once-key**. b163 live @ bb7d1f4 proves it: DOWN race `[201,409,201]` = simultaneous readers collide on seq=1 (1×409) but a serialized reader (count read AFTER a prior commit) gets seq=2 → escapes → 2nd instalment + 2nd receipt JV = double-post. Neither seq-unique (closes only simultaneous) NOR SELECT FOR UPDATE (serializes → seq 1/2/3 → ALL commit) yields "exactly one wins" — that requires an explicit idempotency mechanism. This is the money bug the whole booking/down/deal race work was meant to close; it is NOT closed for down. | (ก) **idempotency key**: web sends a per-submit UUID; server `unique(idempotency_key)` → concurrent identical submits collapse to one 201, rest 409 (general-correct double-submit guard; needs a contract field — likely sacred openapi + web change) · (ข) **first-down-once**: treat the FIRST down as book-once — dedup on a stable `down-initial:<unitId>` key (no seq) so concurrent first-downs collide to one; subsequent legit downs use seq under FOR UPDATE (special-cases first down) · (ค) **downs are distinct**: accept concurrent downs = distinct instalments; relax the b163 DOWN assertion to "no 5xx · N distinct seqs" + rely on web double-submit-disable (SPEC call — needs prototype confirm that duplicate down submission is NOT expected to dedup) · **spec-scout should read `pototype/*sales*`/down flow for the submission/dedup model BEFORE ruling.** ✅ **SPEC-SCOUT DONE (ade802f):** prototype `DownPaymentReceiveForm` makes the client **SELECT the instalment number** — `<Field label="งวดที่"><Select value="งวด 8 จาก 10">` (`sales-process.jsx:465`); down is a fixed **plan of งวด N/M** (`down[]`, progress done/total). Each receipt maps to a PRE-DEFINED instalment slot, not a server-appended row. No idempotency token, no double-submit guard (mock artifact — NOT a licence to double-post). First-down NOT special. → **natural key = `(sales_unit_id, instalment_no)`** (every งวด once — broader than "first-down-once"). Contract `createSalesDown` requestBody = `Entity` (opaque, B-014) → adding an `instalment_no` field is **NOT a sacred openapi change**. Correct fix = backend keys on a CLIENT-PROVIDED `instalment_no` (the selected งวด) + `unique(sales_unit_id, instalment_no)` — a STABLE key → no serialized-reader escape (root was `seq=count+1` server-derived+mutable). Needs orch-A (backend) + likely orch-C (web SalesDown sends the selected งวด). **RECOMMEND (ข-expanded).** ✅ **RESOLVED Wei=ข-ขยาย (2026-07-27):** natural key **`(sales_unit_id, instalment_no)`**, client-provided. **KEY SIMPLIFICATION: migration 0043 `unique(sales_unit_id, seq)` ALREADY EXISTS** → the fix is small: (1) orch-A backend `createSalesDown` reads a CLIENT-provided instalment number from the (opaque `Entity`) body and uses it as `seq` — DROP `seq = count+1` (the mutable server-derive that caused the serialized-escape); validate 1..plan_total; the existing 0043 unique index then closes the race with a STABLE key. (2) orch-C web `SalesDown` sends the selected `งวดที่ N` on submit. NO new migration, NO sacred openapi. Then **orch-B re-runs b163 DOWN live to confirm [201,409,…] → exactly one wins.** ⚠ **+SECOND DEFECT in the SAME handler (P3 pre-promote audit · CONFIRMED high ×2 skeptics + orch-B ground-truth):** `createSalesDown` reads `unit`/`existingDown` OUTSIDE the tx (land-sales.ts:~652/655) then inside the tx overwrites `sales_unit.down` jsonb wholesale with `[...existingDown, new]` (:~713/714). Two concurrent downs both read `existingDown=[X]` → last-writer-wins → the jsonb mirror LOSES an instalment even though both down_payment_txn rows + JVs commit. `listDowns` (GET /sales/downs) reads the JSONB mirror → **under-reports the down schedule (wrong done/total)**. The B-167 seq/instalment_no fix does NOT close this (two DISTINCT legit งวด recorded concurrently still clobber). **FIX IN THE SAME REWORK:** read `existingDown` INSIDE the tx with `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the sales_unit row, OR (cleaner) derive GET /sales/downs from `down_payment_txn` (authoritative) instead of the jsonb mirror. | ✅ **RESOLVED + PROMOTED → main 38f6b7a (22nd 0-drift, 2026-07-27):** backend 6a169b7 (client instalment_no AS seq · drop count+1 · GET reads authoritative down_payment_txn) + web 648dfd5 (งวดที่ selector · sends instalment_no+seq) → dev f09b6f0. PROVEN LIVE: b163 DOWN 3/3 @ f09b6f0 (concurrent SAME instalment_no → exactly one · read-model consistent) · gate-4.5 backend+web PASS (NULL-seq positive-int validation load-bearing). Money double-post CLOSED on main. | 2026-07-27 |

| B-203 | Phase-6 W1b — Full/Enterprise plan `["*"]` menu wildcard vs the edit tree (non-block · fidelity nuance) | The Full/Enterprise package is seeded with `menus:["*"]` (packages.ts L110 · a live-all wildcard the tenant gate + `expandMenus` resolve to every nav id). The ported pkg-builder edit form seeds the tree from `preset.menus` verbatim (prototype-faithful — L116), so editing Full shows a 0-checked tree (`1/total` header) and the `*` has no checkbox to remove. Consequence: an owner who narrows Full by TICKING items saves `["*", ...ticks]` → `expandMenus` still releases ALL menus (the restriction is masked; the post-save card visibly shows all-menus, so it's not silent-corrupt but the tree UX is misleading). | B-274 | mobile offline-UX: dead-end retry on a permanently-failed op + a concurrent-drain "saved" window (lane-wide note) | Two INHERITED behaviours of the shared mobile offline-write pattern, observed while porting pm-checklist. **(1) Dead-end retry affordance:** once an op is dead-lettered the CTA still invites the tap, but the replay loop skips it — `sync_processor.dart:184-187` walks `pending()` (pending + failed) and replays only `SyncOpStatus.pending`, a `failed` op being a permanent 4xx dead-letter — so the retry path (`pm_checklist_screen.dart:230-233` · `pm_checkin_screen.dart:220-223`) re-drains forever with nothing to replay. **(2) Concurrent-drain window:** `_resolve` re-reads `due()` AFTER the drain (`pm_checklist_screen.dart:248-250` · `pm_checkin_screen.dart:226-228`), so a drain racing an in-flight op can report `saved` while that op is still `inFlight`. Both are byte-for-byte the MERGED `pm_checkin` pattern — pm-checklist did not diverge — and both are **money=NONE** (no double-post exposure; money writes are covered by the B-261 idempotency contract). Filed as a lane-wide item rather than fixing one screen out of step with the rest of the mobile lane. | (ก) fix lane-wide in the shared offline layer (gate the CTA on replayable-status + resolve from the post-drain op state) · (ข) leave — money=NONE, cosmetic | (pending) | 🟡 OPEN (inherited · non-blocking · money=NONE) |
| B-276 | web gr.list — over-receipt on one line masks a short line (slice HELD after 3 review rounds) | **Σ-then-assert, one relocation past the two-population class.** `isComplete` (gr-rows.ts) and the ratio/bar are formed from **Σ over the line set**, and `Σreceived >= Σordered` does NOT imply every line met its own. The module already owns the correct per-line predicate (`isItemShort`) and `isComplete` never consults it. Contract-legal payload (openapi puts no `required` on a createGr line; gr.ts rejects only negatives so `qty_ok: 0` persists): lines A `{qty_ok:200, ordered_qty:100}` + B `{qty_ok:0, ordered_qty:100}` → header received 200 == line total 200 and both lines are measured, so **both round-2 gates pass**. Ratio-only variant (A 150/100 + B 50/100) still shows 100%; the `Math.min(100,…)` clamp cannot help because it clamps AFTER summing, hiding the cross-line padding rather than catching it. **NOT seed-reachable** (all 5 seeded GRs fail `isFullyMeasured`) and **not producible by the web writer** (`buildLines` posts one bare unnamed line) — reachable via the documented contract and by any mobile client, the same tier at which the round-2 finding was rated blocking. Existing test *an over-receipt still counts as complete* blesses the hole: both its lines are over-received, so it never exercises masking. | (ก) `isComplete = isFullyMeasured(row) && ordered > 0 && !row.items.some(isItemShort)` **and** clamp the ratio per line (`Σ min(received, ordered) / Σ ordered`) — reviewer-specified, one line each, **orch-B recommends** · (ข) badge-only fix — leaves the 100% bar lying · (ค) withhold both ratio and badge whenever any line is short · (ง) accept: not seed-reachable, revisit when a mobile client posts named lines | **(ก) ADOPTED 2026-08-06 (orch-B, standing order: choose the most honest option and record it).** Implemented verbatim — `isComplete = isFullyMeasured && ordered > 0 && !items.some(isItemShort)` + per-line ratio cap. Slice `feature/web-grlist-rewire` was **HELD, not merged** (3 rework rounds = the debug-protocol cap). The branch is otherwise green: typecheck 0 · the two-population class IS eliminated (17-site inventory + structural fix: the view now prints only `grRowDisplay`/`grItemDisplay`, no raw wire number) · 5 independent revert probes all red. | B-284 | work_period `seq` + `pct` are unvalidated and non-unique — every ordinal/cumulative render in the repo trusts them (web wo.list now withholds; subcon.accept + subcon.progress still trust) | **Root cause is the column contract, not any one screen.** `work_period.seq` is `integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` with **no `unique(contract_id, seq)`** (`packages/db/src/schema/subcon.ts` — the index list is `(contract_id, status)` only) and `work_period.pct` is `numeric(6,3) NOT NULL DEFAULT '0'`. `POST /subcon/contracts` writes both straight through with no validation at all: `seq: toNum(pick(p, "seq")) ?? 0` and `pct: String(toNum(pick(p, "pct")) ?? 0)` (`apps/api/src/routes/subcon.ts` L661/663) — no per-period `seq` required/positive check, no per-period `pct > 0` check, no Σ pct = 100 check. So a client that posts a `periods` array without `seq` persists a plan whose every row is seq 0, duplicate seqs are legal, and a percent-basis plan with an unrecorded share (pct 30 / 0 / 40) or an impossible one (Σ 250) is legal. Reproduced against branch HEAD before the fix: all-seq-0 plan → every installment captioned "ที่ 100% ของสัญญา" and every row labelled ดาวน์; pct 30/0/40 → installment 2's caption byte-identical to installment 1's; Σ 250 → "ที่ 250% ของสัญญา". **web wo.list is fixed defensively** (`cumulativeContractPct` + `hasOrdinalSeq`, per-element guards, withhold → em-dash) but that is a screen-local mitigation of a data-contract hole, and the sweep found two more readers of the same columns that still trust them: `subcon-accept-rows.ts cumMap` (no basis / pct / seq guard, gated only by `periodMethod()` = the FIRST period's basis) and `subcon-progress.tsx` L652 (`p.seq === 0` → ดาวน์). Neither is touched here — each is a different screen with its own G5 baseline, the same boundary under which po.list was left to its own slice. **money = NONE**: nothing here originates or posts an amount; `amount` is the server column and the WO `progress` is server-derived. That was false as written, and the guarding test could not see the string because it only ran after the label had already flipped. The label is now dropped and the button says what it does; **the deviation from the reference image is filed separately as B-285**, and the claim in this row is now true of the shipped screen. **The risk this row exists for is future:** when B-108b implements that stub as a real customer LINE push, (a) saving notes at step 2 would notify the customer before the job is actually finished, and (b) pm-close (step 3) would notify a SECOND time. Second consequence of `…/close` being the only writer, disclosed here rather than left silent: the body must send **all three keys always** (the handler keys off presence, so an omitted key could never CLEAR a field the technician emptied), which makes the save a whole-form overwrite — so a work order the screen could not READ withholds the form and the save entirely, and the offline write is only reachable after a successful read in that session. | B-282 | P1-BE-01 · auth production chain — invite/forgot/reset has NO delivery transport (the seam is a NO-OP) | The chain itself is merged and tested: POST /users provisions a passwordless credential, POST /auth/forgot mints a token, POST /auth/reset consumes it single-use, kills every live session and lets the user log in. **What does not exist is delivery.** `ResetDelivery` (auth-provisioning.ts:296) defaults to a NO-OP and `index.ts:78-89` logs a boot warn saying so, so /auth/forgot answers its uniform 200 and mails nothing, and an invited user (`status: invited`, password NULL) has no way to receive their link — only an admin reset (POST /admin/users/{id}/reset-password) can hand one out, and that also has no transport. B-269 landed the **message** half (`EmailNotificationAdapter` → `SmtpEnvelope` over an injected `SmtpTransport`); the **wire** half is still gated on B-269's own ruling. **HARD CONSTRAINT on whoever wires this — the wiring must ENQUEUE, not await.** /auth/forgot deliberately returns the identical 200 for a known and an unknown address (no account-existence oracle); awaiting an SMTP send inside the handler makes the known-address branch measurably slower and turns that uniform 200 into a **timing oracle that enumerates every registered address**. The send must be handed to a queue/worker and the handler must return without waiting for it. Also: a concurrent `feature/api-email-adapter` branch exists; "nothing in the workspace depends on @juneflow/notifications" is true of dev today but may not stay true — coordinate before wiring. | (ก) wire `ResetDelivery` → the B-269 adapter behind an **enqueue-and-return** hop (handler enqueues, a worker drains and sends) — recommended; the only option that keeps the uniform 200 constant-time · (ข) wire it synchronously inside the request — simplest, but it ships the enumeration oracle described above; recorded only so the trade-off is explicit · (ค) defer until B-269 picks a transport — the chain stays complete but undeliverable; invites are handed out out-of-band | (pending) | 🟡 OPEN — รอ Wei · non-blocking (the chain is merged, audited and unit-proven; delivery is a deliberate seam, and the boot warn says so) |
| B-283 | P1-BE-01 · POST /users invite — `auth_user.email` is UNIQUE platform-wide, not per company | Migration 0008 makes `auth_user.email` unique across the WHOLE platform, but one person legitimately belongs to two tenants (a subcontractor's PM appears in the main contractor's company and in their own). **The blocking behaviour is HELD, not shipped** — say so plainly, because an earlier cut of this slice did ship it. That cut added a platform-wide `findByEmail` pre-check answering 409 "address taken" across tenants; it was removed before merge on two grounds: it decides an unruled product question, and it is a **cross-tenant existence oracle** — any company admin could enumerate which addresses are registered anywhere on the platform. Current behaviour is therefore exactly pre-B-282: the second tenant's invite returns **201**, the dictionary `user` row is written and kept, and `provision()` raises `CredentialEmailTakenError` which the handler catches, logs server-side (users.ts:239-251) and swallows — so the user sits `invited` with **no credential of their own**, and that particular invite cannot be COMPLETED until this is decided (no auth_user in that tenant → no reset link can be issued against it). The same-company duplicate keeps its scoped 409, unchanged. | (ก) `UNIQUE(company_id, email)` via a SACRED migration (drop `auth_user_email_unique`, add the composite) → one auth_user per tenant, the invite completes normally — **recommended, but it needs a login ruling in the same round**: better-auth signs in by email ALONE, so two auth_user rows sharing an address make the login lookup ambiguous and the sign-in path must learn which tenant is meant · (ข) one identity per platform: the second tenant LINKS the existing auth_user instead of provisioning a new one — no migration, but one password then spans both tenants and a tenant admin's reset affects the other tenant's access · (ค) ratify the block and ship the 409 — accepts the cross-tenant existence oracle · (ง) defer — cross-tenant invites keep landing `invited` and uncompletable, as today | (pending) | 🟡 OPEN — รอ Wei · non-blocking (nothing regresses; the cross-tenant invite is uncompletable, which is the pre-B-282 state) |

The merged handlers also answer: reset → **400 INVALID_TOKEN** (auth.ts:394), **400 VALIDATION** (auth.ts:404), **429 RATE_LIMITED** (auth.ts:388); forgot → **429 RATE_LIMITED** (auth.ts:319). The 400 on a dead/expired/replayed token is the endpoint's **primary** failure — the code is right and the contract is short, not the other way round. openapi.yaml is SACRED so nothing was changed here; this row exists so it can be fixed in a contract round. **Impact:** a client generated from the contract has no typed case for the primary failure of the reset endpoint, so the web/mobile reset screens must hand-roll the 400 branch against an untyped response. **G2 does not catch it:** `tests/contract/drift.spec.ts` compares `METHOD path` keys only (drift.spec.ts:93-108) and never inspects declared response codes, so it passes with the gap open. **Precedent:** POST /auth/login already ships an undeclared 429 (auth.ts:250,260) against a 200+401 declaration on dev — pre-existing pattern, not a novel violation. | (ก) declare 400 + 429 on `authForgot`/`authReset` (and login's 429) in a SACRED openapi round, then regen the FE/mobile clients — recommended · (ข) declare only the two 400s, and declare 429 once globally as a shared response since throttling is cross-cutting · (ค) accept the divergence and record it in api-contract.md instead — cheapest, but the generated clients stay blind to it | (pending) | 🟡 OPEN — รอ Wei · non-blocking (handler behaviour is correct; this is a contract-declaration gap only) |

| B-288 | mobile pm-close — signature capture is UNBUILT, so the screen ships READ-ONLY and its close CTA is permanently disabled | **The close cannot be performed honestly, so it is not performed at all — this is the ruling this slice needs.** Chain of ground truth: (1) `POST /pm/workorders/:id/close` writes ONLY the close fields the body carries — cause/fix/advice and `signature` → `customer_sign` (`apps/api/src/routes/pm.ts` **L785-793**); (2) cause/fix/advice belong to **pm-notes** (step 2) and were already saved there through this same endpoint (B-281); pm-close has no form for them; (3) the only field left is the signature, and it **cannot be captured**: `pubspec.yaml` carries no signature package, adding a native dependency is a Wei-level stack decision, and the TEXT column's ENCODING is undefined — nothing in the repo reads `customer_sign`'s content, only whether it is empty (`apps/web/src/screens/pm/wo-rows.ts` **L206**, `pm_jobs_agg`), so inventing an encoding for a column two platforms share is a contract decision, not a screen decision; (4) therefore the body would be **empty**, and the handler then writes nothing at all (`Object.keys(set).length > 0` is false, pm.ts **L796**), fires the no-op `lineNotifyStub` (B-108b) and returns 200 with the row byte-identical. SVG path? data-URI PNG?) so web and mobile agree, since today nothing reads its content. **(ค)** let pm-close POST an empty-bodied close anyway (fires the notify stub, writes nothing) — **rejected in-slice as a fabricated outcome**, listed only for completeness. **(ง)** rule that design fidelity wins on the deviations above and restore the success view / CTA label — note this would make the screen state things no column backs. | | รอ Wei ตอบ — ไม่ block งาน (branch ships; only the close ACTION is withheld) |
| B-289 | B-262 mobile durable offline queue — the durable store has **no `QueryExecutor` on the only build this repo can produce (web)**; vendoring `sqlite3.wasm` + `drift_worker.js` is a stack decision | The rest of B-262 shipped: `AppServices.bootstrap` now opens the drift/SQLite queue (`openDurableSyncQueue`), one shared `QueueDrainProcessor` lives in `AppServices` instead of three screens' `build` methods, and `SyncResumeDrain` supplies the on-mount/on-resume trigger the level-(a) policy doc always promised. **What is NOT shipped is durability on web.** Opening a drift DB needs a `QueryExecutor`. | B-290 | web subcon.accept + subcon.progress + subcon.handover — per-element guards on the `cumMap` cumulative markers, the derived contract method, and every period ordinal (DONE · non-blocking · no G5 re-baseline needed) | **Fixed — this is the web half of B-284 option (ค), applied to the two readers that B-277 (i)/(ii)/(iii) and B-284 both named as still trusting the unvalidated `work_period` columns.** The class: *a claim about ONE period was gated by the FIRST period, by a Σ, or by nothing at all.* Sites found and fixed together: **(1) `subcon-accept-rows.ts cumMap`** had NO precondition whatsoever — it summed whatever `pct` arrived in whatever order `seq` imposed, admitted only by the render gate `method === "percent"` where `deriveMethod()` returns the FIRST period's basis. Now returns `null` (view draws no markers and em-dashes `subcon.progressLegend`, which literally describes those lines) unless: every period is percent-basis · every period's own `pct > 0` · `seq` is a usable ordinal (`hasOrdinalSeq`) · Σpct ≤ 100 (the one Σ-shaped check left — it gates a Σ-shaped fact and disqualifies the whole series uniformly, and stops a marker being positioned at `left:140%` and silently clipped by the bar's `overflow:hidden`). **(2) `deriveMethod`** returned the first period's basis and was read as a CONTRACT fact by four renders (title method chip · percent tracker gate · distance/unit tracker gate · handover certificate method row). `work_period.basis` is a per-ROW column with no contract-level counterpart anywhere in the schema or on the wire, so a mixed plan is contract-legal and used to light the percent tracker over metres. Now returns "" unless every period agrees; callers withhold. **(3) the acceptance table's measure-column HEADER** (subcon-accept.tsx) was picked from `method` while each cell picks from its own `p.basis` — the header-vs-row mismatch; a mixed plan was headed "Milestone". Unknown/mixed now em-dashes the header. **(4) every period ORDINAL render**: subcon-progress.tsx L652 (`p.seq === 0` → ดาวน์ stamped on every row of a defaulted all-zero plan), subcon-accept.tsx period column + acceptance-modal title + the AcceptForm accept/reject toasts (prop `periodSeq: number` → `periodLabel: string`, resolved by the parent — the only place that can see the whole plan), and subcon-handover.tsx certificate rows (checked over the WHOLE plan, not the `accepted` subset, because the printed number claims a position in the contract's plan). All gated on `hasOrdinalSeq`; array position is never substituted, that would fabricate an ordinal. `hasOrdinalSeq` is now ONE implementation shared with wo.list (`po-wo-rows.ts`, param widened to the structural `{ seq }`) rather than a second copy that can drift — both modules narrow the same `work_period` rows. **Display SEMANTICS deliberately untouched**: B-277 Ruling 2 (cumulative share vs own share) is still open and is NOT decided here — cumMap's cumulative reading is byte-identical for any plan that passes the guards. This slice only stops a wrong number being printed; it does not choose which right number to print. **G5**: NO re-baseline needed, correcting the parenthetical in B-284 option (ค) ("moves two G5 baselines") — and consistent with B-284's own option (ง). Every guard PASSES on seed: `packages/db/src/seed/index.ts` writes `seq: si + 1` (distinct, 1-based) and `basis: c.basis` uniformly per contract, and the one percent contract (WO-2026-0042) is pct 20/30/25/25 = 100, so markers stay 20/50/75/100, the legend renders, the method chip renders, the "%"/colQty/colMilestone headers are unchanged, and every ordinal prints its real seq. Pinned by a unit test on the exact seed plan plus screen-seam tests; nothing under `tests/visual/` was touched. The live gate was NOT executed in this slice (no running stack). **money = NONE** — nothing here originates, computes or posts an amount; every figure shown is a server column. | (ก) accept as-is: guards are defence-in-depth on the web side, B-284 still owns the data-contract fix at source · (ข) revert the header em-dash only (item 3) and keep the mixed-plan header as "Milestone" for prototype fidelity · (ค) also withhold the whole percent tracker CARD (not just its markers) when cumMap returns null — costs a prototype element but asserts nothing at all | — | **VERIFIED CLOSED 2026-08-07 by B-317.** `hasOrdinalSeq` is ONE implementation (`po-wo-rows.ts:511`, re-exported by `subcon-accept-rows.ts:62` — not copy-pasted), guards are per-element, and all three readers withhold to an em-dash. B-317's reviewer constructed the three malformed shapes itself against both readers and hunted a fourth (negative pct · null · undefined · NaN · string pct · Σ=0 · empty array · non-integer seq) — all withhold. Two guards that shipped UNPINNED were found and fixed in the same round. | ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-07 |

apply แล้ว en/zh/ar จะติด. ส่วนที่เหลือของ 4 จอเป็น **zero-mint** ทั้งหมด เพราะ web after-sales port ที่ merge แล้วใช้คำเดียวกัน (`sales.service.*`). **คำถาม 2 ข้อ (deviation จาก §0 กฎ 1 — ตัดสินเองไม่ได้):** **(1) หมวดแจ้งซ่อม — ค่าที่ *บันทึก* ยังไม่มี authority จึง WITHHOLD ไว้ (แก้คำอ้างที่ผิดของรอบก่อน)** ⚠️ รอบก่อนแถวนี้เขียนว่า “dict + **seed** ใช้คำยาวเหมือนกัน” — **ไม่จริง 2 ใน 6 คำ** และบรรทัด seed ที่อ้างถึงคือบรรทัดที่หักล้างมันเอง. ข้อเท็จจริงจาก set-diff เชิงโปรแกรมที่รันซ้ำรอบนี้ (สามชุดคำ ไม่ตรงกันสักคู่):  `dict  sales.service.cat*        : ระบบประปา · ระบบไฟฟ้า · ทาสี · หน้าต่าง/ประตู · ระบบแอร์ · พื้น/กระเบื้อง`  `seed  SERVICE_TICKETS (7 แถว)   : ระบบประปา · ระบบไฟฟ้า · ทาสี · หน้าต่าง · ระบบแอร์ · พื้น`  `proto mobile tiles (L63-64)     : ประปา · ไฟฟ้า · ทาสี · ประตู-หน้าต่าง · แอร์ · พื้น/กระเบื้อง`  **SEED ที่ไม่มีใน DICT** = `พื้น` (`packages/db/src/seed/index.ts` L733), `หน้าต่าง` (L730) ‖ **DICT ที่ไม่มีใน SEED** = `พื้น/กระเบื้อง`, `หน้าต่าง/ประตู` ‖ **PROTO ที่ไม่มีใน DICT** = 4 คำ ‖ **คำที่ตรงกันครบทั้งสามชุดมีคำเดียว: `ทาสี`**. **ไม่มีชุดไหนเป็น authority:** (a) ค่าใน dict คือ *ป้ายของการ์ดกราฟ* “หมวดที่แจ้งซ่อมบ่อย” (`pototype/sales-service.jsx` L131-136) ซึ่ง web port ที่ merge แล้ว **ตัดการ์ดนั้นทิ้งทั้งใบ** ในฐานะ mock analytics ที่ไม่มี wire (`apps/web/src/screens/sales/sales-service.tsx` L49-51) — และ **ไม่มีโค้ดที่ merge แล้วอ่าน 6 คีย์นี้เลย** (grep ทั้ง apps/ + packages/ เจอเฉพาะไฟล์ dict เอง); (b) ค่าใน seed คือ mock 7 แถวที่ลอกจาก prototype ใบเดียวกัน โดย 2 คำสั้นกว่าป้ายกราฟของตัวเอง (อ่านเป็นคำที่ถูกตัดหาง); (c) `service_ticket.category` เป็น **free text** ไม่มี enum ไม่มี endpoint list — handler เก็บค่าที่ client ส่งมาตรง ๆ (`apps/api/src/routes/sales-service.ts` L246) — และ **ฟอร์ม create ของ web ที่ merge แล้วเป็น `<input>` พิมพ์อิสระ** (sales-service.tsx L830) ส่วน register render ค่าดิบ (L451 / L708) ⇒ **ไม่มีที่ไหนในระบบประกาศ vocabulary ไว้เลย**. **ที่ ship อยู่ตอนนี้ (แก้แล้วรอบนี้):** srv-new ยังวาด tile ครบ 6 ช่องตาม prototype (§0 กฎ 1) แต่เป็น **honest-disabled** (โทนป้ายจาง กดไม่ได้ ไม่มี GestureDetector) และ **create ไม่ส่งคีย์ `category` เลย** ⇒ คอลัมน์เป็น NULL แทนที่จะเป็นคำที่เดาเอา. พอ Wei ตัดสิน เปิดกลับแก้จุดเดียว: ส่งค่าที่ตัดสินแล้วเข้า `newTicketBody(category:)` ซึ่งรับพารามิเตอร์รออยู่แล้ว. **(2) ป้าย status ซ้อนกันเองใน prototype** — pill ของ `scheduled` ที่ tech-jobs เขียน `นัดแล้ว` (L238) แต่ timeline ที่ srv-track เรียกสถานะเดียวกันว่า `นัดช่าง` (L142); prototype ขัดกันเอง. slice นี้ใช้ `sales.service.statusScheduled` ('นัดช่าง') ทั้งสองจอ ไม่ mint synonym. **Non-blocking:** ทั้ง 4 จอ merge ได้ตามสภาพนี้ (gate เขียว, ภาษาไทยถูกต้อง) — รอคำตอบเพื่อ apply mint + ล็อกคำหมวด | (1) **(ก)** ใช้ค่า dict `sales.service.cat*` (คำยาว) เป็นค่าที่บันทึก — ตรงป้ายกราฟ prototype · **แต่ชนกับ seed 2 แถว** (SR-2026-0046 `หน้าต่าง`, SR-2026-0043 `พื้น`) ⇒ ต้อง re-seed 2 แถวนั้นด้วย ไม่งั้นแตกอยู่ดี  **(ข)** mint คำสั้นของ mobile 4 คำ แล้วใช้ทั้งแสดงผลและบันทึก — ตรง prototype mobile 100% แต่ไม่ตรงทั้ง seed และป้ายกราฟ web  **(ค)** mint คำสั้นไว้ **แสดงผลอย่างเดียว** แล้ว map เป็นค่ายาวตอนบันทึก — ตรง prototype + สอดคล้อง dict แต่ยังชน seed 2 แถวเหมือน (ก) และต้องเลี้ยงตาราง map ตลอดไป  **(ง)** ประกาศ vocabulary กลางชุดเดียว (เลือกชุดใดชุดหนึ่ง) แล้ว **re-seed ให้ตรง** + เปลี่ยนฟอร์ม create ของ web จาก `<input>` อิสระเป็น list ชุดเดียวกัน — จบทั้งระบบชุดเดียว แต่แตะ seed + จอ web (งานนอกเขต slice นี้)  **(จ)** คง **withhold** ตามที่ ship อยู่ (mobile ไม่เขียน `category`) — **ที่ ship อยู่** · ข้อมูลไม่แตกแน่นอน แต่ฟอร์ม mobile ยังเก็บหมวดไม่ได้จนกว่าจะตัดสิน  ‖ (2) **(ก)** ใช้ `statusScheduled` ทั้งสองจอ — **ที่ ship อยู่** · **(ข)** mint `นัดแล้ว` เป็น pill เฉพาะ tech-jobs ตาม prototype | (pending) | **APPLIED — verified 2026-08-06 by diffing every staged value against the sacred file (not by reading the board): all present, en=zh=ar=th, dict untouched.** All 16 phrases applied ✅; the 2 fidelity questions (category vocabulary · scheduled-pill synonym) remain OPEN for Wei — the mint was never what blocked them. → 🟡 OPEN — รอ Wei · ไม่ block งาน (จอ render ไทยถูกอยู่แล้ว) |

| B-299 | P1-MOB · mobile `exec` — the hero + all four KPI tiles have NO feed; the S-curve legend needs a BAC the /evm response omits; the per-row approve button dropped | Four separate honesty gaps on one screen, all shipped as em-dashes/drops rather than guesses. **(1) The hero (L657-661)** — cumulative sales, units-sold ratio and YoY delta: no endpoint serves any of them. `/sales/*` are per-row LIST endpoints (`land-sales.ts` L925-930), so producing `284.5 M` would mean SUMMING booking rows client-side = originating money. **(2) The KPI tiles (L666-669)** — `กระแสเงินสด 7 วัน` IS served by `GET /dashboard/cashflow-forecast` (`dashboard.ts` L865) but that endpoint is outside this slice's three feeds, so it is a WIRING gap not a backend one; `Project ใกล้กำหนด` and `ขายเดือนนี้` have no endpoint at all; `เอกสารรออนุมัติ` is em-dashed for a subtler reason — the only pending count available (`/dashboard/approvals-inbox`) is TIER-FILTERED to what the caller may act on, so it means "awaiting MY approval", not "awaiting approval". The prototype itself treats them as different quantities (tile 17 vs section 3), so showing the caller's subset under the unscoped label would understate the backlog. The REAL count is shown where it belongs — on the section that says `รออนุมัติของฉัน`. **(3) The S-curve legend (L690-691)** `แผน 65%` / `จริง 62%`: percent-complete in EVM is EV/BAC, and `bac` IS a real stored column (`packages/db/src/schema/finance.ts` L922, "the project total") that `loadEvmSeries` already loads (`evm-series.ts` L49) — but `GET /boq/reports/evm` does not put it in the response body (`series`/`spi`/`cpi`/`currency_code` only). One added field would make both percentages real. **(4) The per-row APPROVE button (L708)** is dropped: this is a read-only screen with no approval-tier logic (that lives on the merged approve/reject sheets), so a button labelled `อนุมัติ` would promise an action it cannot perform. **(5) THE S-CURVE RENDERS ITS EMPTY STATE ON THE SEEDED STACK — proven LIVE, not inferred.** `/dashboard/summary` with no `?project_id` resolves the caller's PRIMARY project as first-by-(`created_at ASC, id ASC`) (`dashboard.ts` resolvePrimaryProject). The seed inserts all 7 projects in ONE statement and `project.created_at` is `defaultNow()` (Postgres `now()` = the transaction timestamp), so all 7 share one identical `created_at` and the tiebreak is `id ASC` → `13ed4a49-…` = **BBT** (`juneflow บางบัวทอง`). `evm_snapshot` is seeded **only** for `project:rjp` (12 rows, `seed/index.ts`). Verified against a freshly migrated + seeded stack this round: grouping `evm_snapshot` by project returns RJP=12 and all six others=0; `GET /dashboard/summary` → `{"project_id":"13ed4a49-…","project_name":"juneflow บางบัวทอง"}`; and the screen's REAL request `GET /boq/reports/evm?project_id=13ed4a49-…` → `{"series":[],"spi":null,"cpi":null,"currency_code":"THB"}`. So the one block on this screen that is fully wired is EMPTY for every seeded caller. **Dropping the project scope does not fix it** — an unscoped read merges every project's snapshots into one list (`evm-series.ts` loadEvmSeries) and would interleave two projects' PV/EV under one period key, drawing a curve that belongs to no project; it only looks correct on this seed by the accident that exactly one project has EVM data. WHAT SHIPS: the scope is kept (it is the same project the rest of the dashboard describes), the card now NAMES that project from `summary.project_name`, and the empty state is an em-dash beneath that name — a true statement about BBT rather than an unattributed blank implying the business has no progress. WHAT IS ESCALATED: which project an EXECUTIVE dashboard should chart at all. Also filed here: the hero gradient's dark stop `#133E6B` (L656) has NO design token, so `brandHover` is used — same direction, lighter stop, a visible deviation from `tests/visual/reference/mobile/exec.png`. | **(1)+(2):** (ก) leave em-dashed until a sales/deadline feed exists [proposed — shipped] · (ข) wire `GET /dashboard/cashflow-forecast` into tile 1 in a follow-up slice (smallest real win) · (ค) build a sales-summary endpoint (cumulative + month + units + YoY) and a due-soon project count. **(2) tile 2 specifically:** (ก) keep em-dashed [proposed — shipped] · (ข) show the caller's tier-filtered count under the unscoped label · (ค) add a tenant-wide pending-doc count endpoint. **(3):** (ก) add `bac` (and/or a server-computed `pct_complete`) to the /evm response body, then wire both legend percentages [proposed] · (ข) leave em-dashed. **(4):** (ก) keep dropped [proposed — shipped] · (ข) make each row NAVIGATE to the existing approve sheet · (ค) build an inline approve with the tier logic. **(5):** (ก) keep the primary-project scope, named on the card, and let the empty state stand — it is honest, and the curve draws the day BBT has snapshots [proposed — shipped] · (ข) give the exec dashboard a PROJECT PICKER so the user chooses the curve's subject instead of inheriting the API's default (adds a control the prototype does not have) · (ค) add a portfolio-level EVM to the server (a real cross-project aggregate — it must be computed SERVER-side; summing project series in the client would be originating money) and chart that on an executive screen · (ง) seed `evm_snapshot` for more than one project so the default dashboard has a curve (makes the demo look right without changing what the screen claims — does NOT address the product question). **Gradient:** (ก) keep `brandHover` [proposed — shipped] · (ข) add a token for `#133E6B` and regenerate. |  | 🟡 OPEN — รอ Wei · NON-BLOCKING (the shipped screen claims nothing false: every gap renders an em-dash; the approvals card is fully wired down to the SERVER's envelope `total` and the SERVER's `currency_code`; the S-curve is fully wired but EMPTY for the seeded primary project — see gap (5), which is a data/product question, not a broken read. `exec_agg.dart` `ExecKpis` records the per-slot reason in typed form, `exec_screen_test.dart` asserts no mock literal reaches the glass, and `exec_scurve_painter_test.dart` pins the drawn geometry to the served series). |

| B-295 | mobile field-pr (feature/mobile-field-group) — the quick-PR write is ONLINE ONLY because an offline replay would create a DUPLICATE purchase requisition | `POST /pr` takes a **client-supplied `no`** (apps/api/src/routes/pr.ts L364) and guards duplicates with a **read-then-insert check** (L399-405: it selects the tenant's PRs and compares `no` in JS). There is no unique index behind it — `packages/db/src/schema/boq.ts` declares `prs.no` as plain `text().notNull()`. So the guard is a race, not a constraint, and the failure mode it leaves open is exactly the one the offline queue would trigger: a create that reaches the server, whose RESPONSE is lost, replays through the SyncProcessor and **creates a second PR** for the same site request. No client-side dedup closes it — the second request is indistinguishable from a legitimate one at the point where it matters. **Shipped behaviour:** this screen is NOT enrolled in the offline queue (the one place it departs from the merged pm-checkin / pm-checklist / pm-notes precedent); it submits online and surfaces a real failure. It also surfaces a THIRD outcome the two-step flow makes possible — `POST /pr` succeeded and `POST /pr/{id}/submit` did not — as a distinct 'saved as draft' state (`pr.form.draftSavedToast`) whose retry re-submits the EXISTING id and never re-creates, because reporting that as a plain failure is precisely what would make a requester raise the same PR twice. The submit step is separately safe: it is a guarded transition only a `draft` PR passes (pr.ts L518-523), so a repeat 409s. **This is a backend + Wei item, not a mobile one** — the fix is the B-261 client-idempotency-key contract applied to `POST /pr`. | (ก) **[recommended · matches B-261]** apply the B-261 client-key template to `POST /pr` (a client-supplied idempotency key + a unique index + a tx-catch on the constraint name, per the B-263 hardening), then mobile enrols this write in the offline queue in a follow-up round · (ข) add `unique(company-scope, no)` on `pr` alone — closes the duplicate but turns a replay into a 409 the client cannot tell from a genuine collision · (ค) keep field-pr online-only permanently and document it as a product decision · (ง) Wei decides |  | **CLOSED 2026-08-07 — informational, and its subject shipped.** The field-pr online-only decision (pr.no has no unique index, so an offline replay would create a duplicate requisition) is merged and recorded in the slice that made it, dev `044f7f0`. Nothing here was ever an action item; it was the reasoning behind a deliberate refusal to call a write offline-safe. | ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-07 |
| B-296 | mobile FOREMAN/FIELD group (fm-accept · field-progress · field-pr) — 9 net-new phrase-layer strings need a sacred i18n round ✅ **APPLIED** | The three ported screens carry **9 distinct Thai strings that exist nowhere in `docs/extract/i18n-full.json`** — verified programmatically against dev (1065 phrases · 3701 dict entries): absent from the phrases layer AND from every `dict.*.th` value. Original options: (ก) **[recommended · matches B-247/B-280]** Wei runs the sacred round from `agents/orch-d-recon/field-group-i18n.apply.json` (th verbatim; en/zh/ar left to the translator per the en=zh=ar=th fallback convention) and regenerates the mobile asset · (ข) hold the mint and keep the Thai-only phrase-as-key fallback (screens stay correct in th, blank-equivalent in en/zh/ar) · (ค) Wei decides |  | ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-06 — applied under standing pre-approval 1 |
| B-297 | mobile FOREMAN/FIELD group — the wire gaps and the two deliberate deviations from the prototype (one ruling asked per item) | Enumerated so none is decided quietly. **(1) fm-accept — the REJECT ACTION is WITHHELD, and goods-receipt rows are READ-ONLY.** **(1a) The one-tap reject is not shipped.** `rejected` is a TERMINAL work-period state: every write of `work_period.status` in subcon.ts is at L666 (`pending`, on create), L784 (`delivered`), L845 (`passed`), L891 (`rejected`), L1020/L1037 (`paid`) — there is NO transition out of `rejected`, and every door refuses it (deliver needs `pending` L754-759 · inspect needs `delivered` / `inspecting` L819-826 · approve-payment needs `passed` L951-957). `POST /defects/{id}/recheck` (L1091-1120) closes a DEFECT row and never touches the period. Meanwhile this screen has NO defect form, so the only reject it could send carries an EMPTY `defects[]`, and the handler's own loop drops every entry with no `item` (L864-871) — zero defect rows written. One unconfirmed tap would therefore permanently fail a subcontractor's work period, with no record of why and nothing able to undo it. The prototype offers no way out either: its reject button (mobile-field.jsx L177) only toasts `ตีกลับ + บันทึก Defect (จากมือถือ)` — it CLAIMS a Defect List was recorded while capturing nothing, the mock mechanic §0 rule 3 forbids reproducing, and inventing a defect form the prototype does not have is forbidden by §0 rule 1. Shipped behaviour: the button keeps its place in the prototype's two-button row as an honest-DISABLED affordance with no tap handler (the merged pm-close B-288 sticky-bar precedent), and the reject path is removed from the code — `kInspectPassPayload` is the only body the slice can build and the repository exposes only `inspectPass()`, so no future caller can open the door by accident. The merged WEB port DOES send a real Defect List (apps/web/src/screens/subcon/accept-form.tsx) because it has an inspection checklist to derive one from; this screen has none. **(1b) goods-receipt rows are read-only.** The prototype filters the acceptance centre to `subcon` + `gr` and renders pass/reject on BOTH (mobile-field.jsx L148/L176-177), but its buttons only toast. `POST /periods/{id}/inspect` takes a WORK-PERIOD id; a receipt's decisions are different doors with different meanings (`POST /gr/{id}/return` / `/cancel`, gr.ts L646/L692). The rows still show (they are genuinely in the queue) with no actions. An already-`rejected` period is withheld for the same reason — the endpoint's own C3 guard would 409 (subcon.ts L819-826). **(2) fm-accept — the money slot is an em-dash.** The wire carries `amount` + `currency_code`, but for 3 of the 4 bases that column is NOT the payable (subcon.ts computeGross: percent → pct/100 × contract.value; distance / unit → per_period_qty × rate_per_unit; only milestone reads the stored amount), `contract.value` is not on this wire, and the gross is server-computed inside `approve-payment` — a different action this screen must not imply it performs. `รอ {n} วัน` (L166), the bold description line (L168) and the `📎` count (L170) are em-dashed too: no wire column. **(3) fm-accept — a 409 and a network failure look the same.** Both render `admin.common.actionFailedToast`, then the queue is re-read so the row's TRUE status shows. A distinct 'another inspector already handled this' message would need a new key. **(4) field-progress — the percentage is withheld.** A work period carries a STATUS, not a completion percentage; its `pct` column is the period's TARGET share under the percent basis, not progress. No ratio is derived from a count of delivered periods either — periods are unequal by construction (that is what the four bases mean), so numerator and denominator would not measure the same quantity. The bar and both delta lines (L328-334) are dropped and the real `status` renders in their place — as its Thai LABEL, never the wire value: `pending` / `delivered` / `inspecting` / `passed` / `rejected` / `paid` are English machine codes and this is a Thai-only field app (§0 rule 2), so the 6 statuses resolve through 4 EXISTING dict keys, ZERO-MINT, using the merged web port's own collapse (subcon-accept-rows.ts mapPeriodStatus + subcon-accept.tsx BADGE_LABEL) so the two clients cannot describe the same period differently: pending→subcon.statusNotReached · delivered + inspecting→subcon.statusRequested · passed + paid→subcon.kpiAccepted · rejected→subcon.rejectBtn. One deliberate departure from the web: an UNKNOWN status renders an em-dash instead of being folded into 'ยังไม่ถึง' — 'not reached yet' is a claim, and a status this build does not know is not evidence for it. **(5) field-progress — the photo grid and the note section are dropped.** The app has no image picker or upload seam AND `GET /subcon-contracts/{id}/periods` returns no acceptance, so no photo can be captured or read; and neither `work_period` nor `acceptance` has a note column anywhere in the schema. Each is an affordance with nothing behind it in either direction. `deliver` therefore always sends `{docs: [], photos: []}` — safe today because the handler only accepts a `pending` period and an acceptance is only ever created BY a deliver/inspect, so there is none to overwrite. **(6) field-progress — a contract PICKER was added.** The prototype pins one contract/period from mock data (L319) and the shell has no route-param mechanism. Rather than ship a screen that can never address a real period, a bare tab route lists the tenant's REAL contracts (`GET /subcon-contracts`) and tapping one loads its periods; a pushed contract id still skips the picker. Nothing is pre-selected. **(7) field-pr — a DOCUMENT-NUMBER field was added, and it has no prototype counterpart.** `POST /pr` requires a client-supplied `no` and there is no issuer endpoint — `GET /doc-numbering` returns the tenant's counters and its own header says issuing-time semantics 'land with the Phase-2 numbering service' (doc-numbering.ts L18-19). Generating one client-side would invent a business identifier the tenant's numbering series owns, so the requester enters it; a collision surfaces the server's 409 honestly. **(8) field-pr — `type` is fixed to `material`.** The prototype offers no type control and the screen is 'quick purchase of a BOQ line from site'; the constant is named in one place (`kFieldPrType`) rather than buried in a request body. **(9) field-pr — the approval-chain card is DROPPED, not em-dashed.** mobile-screens.jsx L467 states `ส่ง → หน.จัดซื้อ → ผจก.โครงการ → ผอ.ก่อสร้าง (วงเงิน ≥ 100K)`; the implemented tiers are ผจก.โครงการ > 500,000 and MD > 2,000,000 (pr.ts, B-070). Rendering it would tell the requester something FALSE about their own document — a claim, not a missing value. The urgency chips and the photo/reason box keep their labels over an em-dash (`pr` has no urgency, photo or reason column; mapping 'ด่วน · 2 วัน' onto `need_date` would invent the mapping as well as the date). **(10) All three screens deviate from `tests/visual/reference/mobile/{fm-accept,field-progress,field-pr}.png`** exactly where the items above withhold or drop mock content — flagged for the G5 round, not silently. | (ก) **[recommended]** ratify items 1a-5 and 8-10 as the correct honest reading (they follow the merged pm-notes B-285 precedent: a missing value is em-dashed, a false claim is dropped), and rule separately on **(6)** the field-progress contract picker and **(7)** the field-pr document-number field, which are the only two ADDITIONS · (ข) revert (6) to the merged approve/reject/pm-checkin honest-empty precedent — the screen then renders 'nothing selected' permanently until a push seam exists · (ค) revert (7) and block PR creation from mobile until a numbering endpoint exists (field-pr becomes a read-only BOQ browser) · (ง) Wei rules per item |  | **CLOSED 2026-08-07 — informational.** The FOREMAN/FIELD group's wire gaps and its two deliberate prototype deviations are enumerated in the merged slice (dev `044f7f0`) and in `agents/journal/mobile.md`. The screens ship; the row exists so the deviations were never silent. | ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-07 |
| B-275 | web gr.list re-wire · prototype "partial" badge has no i18n key | The gr.list re-wire (B-078/F1 data-completeness) restores the received-vs-ordered cell, so the prototype's TWO row badges (`gr.jsx` L82-83) are now derivable: `complete` (received >= ordered) and `partial` (received < ordered). Only the complete badge has a key — `gr.list.badgeComplete`. The prototype renders the partial one as the bare English literal `partial`, which the i18n extraction never captured, so there is NO key for it in `docs/extract/i18n-full.json` (verified: no dict/phrase entry whose value is `partial`; the 6 `*artial*` hits are unrelated sentences). §0 rule 2 forbids inventing a key or hardcoding the literal, so **the complete badge ships and the partial badge is omitted**. Cosmetic only — the same information is already visible in the received/ordered cell (`120 / 240` + a half-filled progress bar), so nothing is hidden from the user and no value is fabricated. | (ก) mint one phrase key for the literal `partial` in a later sacred i18n round, then render the badge · (ข) accept the omission permanently — the received/ordered cell + bar already convey partial-ness, badge is redundant · (ค) allow the bare ASCII literal `partial` in .tsx as a non-translatable token (the i18n-guard only blocks Thai, so it would pass mechanically) | (pending) | 🟢 OPEN — non-blocking, cosmetic (screen ships; complete badge renders, partial omitted, no fabrication) |
| B-270 | contract · POST /gr line `ordered_qty` is optional AND defaults to `qty_ok` — a line can claim completeness it cannot prove | `packages/contracts/openapi.yaml:1609-1622` declares the createGr line object with NO `required` list, and the handler defaults the ordered quantity to the received one (`apps/api/src/routes/gr.ts:451` — `toNum(pick(line,"ordered_qty","orderedQty")) ?? qtyOk`). A client that posts a named line WITHOUT `ordered_qty` therefore reaches the GET /gr wire as `ordered_qty == received_qty`, **byte-identical to a genuinely fully-received line** — so gr.list cannot tell "received everything that was ordered" from "nobody ever said what was ordered", and any completeness rendering (the `gr.list.badgeComplete` badge, the 100% bar) would be asserting a fact the payload does not contain. Not fixable client-side: no other field on the GET /gr wire (nor GET /po / GET /wo, which expose only no/status/amount) carries an independent ordered quantity. **Scope of the web-side mitigation already shipped (feature/web-grlist-rewire):** the badge now additionally requires every line to carry `ordered_qty > 0` and the header total to equal the line total (no unmeasured bare-line quantity) — `isFullyMeasured()` in `apps/web/src/screens/gr/gr-rows.ts`. That closes the bare-line route to a false badge; it CANNOT close this defaulted-ordered_qty route. **Round-3 addendum (same contract latitude, second variant):** because no line field is required, a client may also persist an EXPLICIT `ordered_qty: 0` — the `?? qtyOk` default fires only when the field is ABSENT — i.e. a named line carrying received quantity with no order to measure it against. Summing such a receipt then compares a superset (every line's received) with a subset (only the measured lines' ordered) — the same defect as the header-vs-items pairing, one level down. The web side now treats an unmeasured line as unmeasurable everywhere: no ratio, no bar, no fully-received label, no badge (`allLinesMeasured()` / `itemMeasure()` in `gr-rows.ts`). The wire still cannot say whether that 0 means "nothing was ordered" or "nobody recorded it"; option (ข) below (NULL, not 0) is the only one that resolves BOTH variants. the work is on `feature/b305-sacred-commit-guard` (`3ae5674`) and is NOT merged. **Round 1** (PreToolUse Bash, commit-text scan) — broken 3 ways: `bash script.sh` containing a commit (the original incident, since the hook sees the command TEXT and the commit is inside a file), `git revert` of an approved sacred commit, and `merge`/`cherry-pick`/`git am`. **Round 2** (git's own `prepare-commit-msg` + `pre-applypatch`, tracked via relative `core.hooksPath`, with a `Sacred-Override:` trailer) closed all three — verified — but was broken 3 MORE ways: **(a) the trailer is unauthenticated** — `git commit -m "..." -m "Sacred-Override: wei-approved:B-999"` is accepted, the id need not exist, nothing reads BLOCKERS.md, and a minted trailer is byte-identical to a forged one so the "permanent greppable receipt" cannot distinguish them; **(b) `git am --no-verif`** — git accepts unambiguous prefixes while the filter tested `== "--no-verify"`; **(c) the control does not protect itself** — `.claude/**` is not in the sacred list, so `sed -i '1a exit 0' .claude/hooks/git/prepare-commit-msg` followed by the unchanged `full-rebaseline.sh` put **50 files under `tests/visual/reference/**` into HEAD**, i.e. the original incident reproduced verbatim. **THE STRUCTURAL CONCLUSION:** every layer available here — Claude hooks, git hooks, commit trailers — is a FILE the agent can edit, so an in-repo control can only ever raise the cost of a bypass, never prevent one. A real control has to sit where the agent cannot reach: a server-side `pre-receive` hook, branch protection, or a human reviewing before promote. **The last of those already exists and is what actually protects main today — Wei promotes alone, and nothing this session touched main.** Two facts the rework measured that contradict common belief and are worth keeping: `cherry-pick` and `revert` do NOT run `pre-commit`, and `--no-verify` does NOT skip `prepare-commit-msg`. | ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-07 (limits documented · branch unmerged) |

After the aborted race: **0 ledger rows, 0 JVs, on-hand untouched** = real atomicity. SACRED migration.  **(ก) DONE 2026-08-07 — Wei ruled, merged to dev `ed0f8eb`.** migration **0061** = `CREATE UNIQUE INDEX (company_id, no)` on BOTH `ap_deposit` and `jv`; FULL not partial because both columns are NOT NULL on both tables (the B-307 NULL-escape does not apply, and a predicate would only add one). Retry gated on the constraint NAME via `violatedConstraint`. **Reproduced live first:** 6 concurrent distinct requests → 6 deposits collapsed onto ONE number and `JV-2026-0460` six times; after the fix the same burst gives 6/6 × 201 with 6 distinct numbers and zero duplicate JVs. **The most likely fake-fix was checked directly and is not one:** in Postgres a failed statement aborts the transaction, so a naive retry can never succeed — but 15 of the 17 JV insert sites allocate OUTSIDE the transaction and `TenantDb.transaction` delegates to the root pool, so each retried `db.transaction()` is top-level on a fresh connection. The reviewer forced a permanent collision: **all 10 attempts reached the DB (10 server-log records) and `grep 25P02` over the whole Postgres log was EMPTY** — an aborted-transaction retry would have died before touching the table. Two sites needed a different anchor and got one: `fa.ts` allocates INSIDE the tx (retry wraps the whole `postDepreciation` call) and `gl.ts` increments a JS counter for a batch (re-bases mid-batch from the committed max → 0435/0437/0438/0439, no dupes, no gaps). **Sequential numbering survives** — when a JV collided but the deposit did not, `DP-2026-0005` was RE-USED not skipped. Exhaustion bounded at 10 attempts → **503 `RETRY`, never the bare 409** below it, and the reason is verified: `sync_processor.dart:227` dead-letters 4xx permanently and defers 5xx, so a 409 would make the phone discard work it should retry. All 16 production call sites carry the exhaustion branch first. api 1426→**1448**. **OPERATOR STEP, not code:** the index CANNOT be built on a stack where the defect already fired (proven — clean on a fresh seed, aborts on the stack that had just reproduced it). **No de-dup DELETE was added** — these are money rows with balanced JV legs, and renumbering a posted voucher is Wei's decision.
