| B-330 | mobile offline writes — the client idempotency key did NOT survive an app kill on all FIVE offline-write screens; `st-receive` is a MONEY path (fix APPLIED on `feature/mobile-opid-rehydrate`, client-only, no sacred file — retroactive confirmation + 2 follow-ups needing ids) | **THE DEFECT.** Every offline-write screen held its client idempotency key in a plain `String? _opId` State field and **nothing in `initState` rehydrated it** (`field_progress_screen.dart:173` · `st_receive_screen.dart:195` · `pm_checkin_screen.dart:139` · `pm_notes_screen.dart:197` · `pm_checklist_screen.dart:159`; the on-mount code called only `repo.drain()`). State is not durable and the drift/SQLite queue is, so: **submit while offline → op `k1` enqueued, drain defers it, op stays `pending` → app killed → user reopens the screen, still offline → the on-mount drain defers `k1` again and `_opId` is null → the user taps again → a FRESH key `k2` is minted and a SECOND op enqueued.** When the signal returns both replay and the server writes **two rows**. No server-side guard can help: a partial unique index on the key is doing its job — two distinct keys are legitimately two distinct records — so the duplicate has to be prevented on the client by not minting the second key. `st-receive` writes `POST /gr`, which posts a GL journal voucher, so its duplicate is a **duplicated goods receipt AND a duplicated JV**; the B-261/B-263/B-264 idempotency contract was correct and simply never reached, because the client stopped honouring it at the process boundary. **And a comment asserted the opposite** (`st_receive_screen.dart:190-194`: the key is "REUSED on every manual retry") — true within a session, FALSE across a restart. It now states the restart behaviour explicitly, including what used to happen. **WHAT THE QUEUE'S IDENTITY MODEL ACTUALLY IS** (this is what decides whether the fix is safe). `SyncQueue.pending()` is ONE GLOBAL FIFO across every screen, ordered by `createdAt`, returning `pending` + `failed`. It carries **no notion of ownership** — ops are keyed only by `SyncOperation.id`, the caller-minted key. Ownership therefore has to be reconstructed from what the op itself carries: `entityType` + `endpoint`, where for four of the five the record id is IN the path (`/pm/workorders/{id}/checkin` · `.../close` · `.../checklist`, `/periods/{id}/deliver`). **`st-receive` is the exception: `POST /gr` is the same endpoint for every PO** and the anchor travels in the BODY as `po_id`, so matching on entity type + endpoint alone would let PO-B's screen adopt PO-A's key — which on a money path means **PO-B's receipt is silently never sent at all** (the mirror-image loss, not a duplicate). **THE FIX (client-only, zero sacred files, no contract or payload change).** New shared `apps/mobile/lib/offline/pending_op_adoption.dart`: `SyncOpIdentity` (entityType + endpoint + optional `payloadAnchor`) plus `findAdoptableOp` / `findAdoptableOpAmong`. Each repository exposes ONE identity function (`stReceiveOpIdentity` / `pmCheckinOpIdentity` / `pmNotesOpIdentity` / `pmChecklistOpIdentity` / `fieldDeliverOpIdentity`) and **builds its `SyncOperation` from that same expression**, so the matcher cannot silently drift away from the builder — the `formatQty`-triplication trap, closed by construction rather than by discipline. On mount each screen awaits the (a) drain FIRST (online → the queue clears → nothing to adopt), then adopts whatever is still pending for ITS record and shows the honest `queued` card. Two rules live in the shared helper instead of being restated five times: **(1) only `pending` ops are adoptable — a 4xx dead-letter is skipped by every future drain, so adopting one would strand the user on a write that can never be sent and never be re-made**; **(2) a screen adopts only its own op.** **FOUR OF THE FIVE ARE THE SAME SHAPE; `field-progress` LEGITIMATELY IS NOT** (checked, not assumed — it was not forced into the common shape). The other four have ONE anchor fixed for the whole mount, so they adopt straight after the drain. `field-progress` resolves its anchor from an async read, shows N periods at once, and lets the foreman switch contracts without leaving the screen — so its adoption is tied to a completed `_load()` (and re-runs on `_selectContract`), and an op belonging to a period of another contract is left alone rather than raising a status bar about an off-screen row. It also exposed a SECOND, pre-existing bug of the same class **that needs no restart at all**: the screen has ONE `_opId` slot but many periods, so delivering period B leaves A's op in the queue but not in the slot, and a second tap on A minted a third key. `_deliver` now asks the QUEUE before minting — the slot cannot answer that question and never could. `pm_notes` needed one further ordering care: `_load` seeds the three controllers, which fires `_onEdited`, which legitimately DROPS the op id (a typed character is a new body). The read is therefore started in parallel but AWAITED before adoption, or the adoption would be undone nondeterministically. **VERIFICATION.** `flutter analyze` clean · `flutter test` **828** (baseline 801; +27 = 16 helper unit tests + 11 restart/ownership/boundary widget tests). The new widget tests deliberately do NOT reuse the screens' existing fake repositories: every one of those returns `due() => const []`, i.e. "nothing is ever queued", which cannot express this defect at all. They drive the REAL repositories (only the READ methods stubbed, on subclasses), the REAL `QueueDrainProcessor` policy, and a REAL queue that OUTLIVES the widget tree — the exact durability asymmetry that made `_opId` lossy. **REVERT PROBE, run per screen because one red is not proof five are covered:** st_receive rehydration removed → **1 red** (restart/st-receive) · pm_checkin → **1 red** · pm_notes → **2 red** · pm_checklist → **2 red** · field_progress mount adoption → **1 red** · field_progress pre-mint queue check → **1 red**. Three further probes on the MATCHER, since a too-loose matcher is worse than no fix: drop `st-receive`'s payload anchor → **1 red** (the PO-A/PO-B money test) · ignore the endpoint → **6 red** · adopt dead-letters too → **4 red**. B-327's known flake (`sync_resume_drain_test.dart`) did not fire in any run and is untouched by this change — nothing here alters drain timing. **TWO FOLLOW-UPS FOUND HERE, RECORDED RATHER THAN SILENTLY FIXED — each needs an id allocated at merge time (not grabbed here: parallel slices have collided on self-assigned ids before).** **(F1) the form shows read values while the QUEUE holds what was submitted.** B-330 restores the op id and the `queued` card, which is what stops the duplicate; it does NOT restore form CONTENT from the queued payload (out of the stated scope, and a per-screen job). So a relaunched `st-receive` re-seeds counts from the ordered quantities while the queued receipt may say 760/800 — the CTA correctly re-drains the QUEUED payload (760), but the screen displays 800. Same shape on `pm-notes`, `pm-checklist`, and `pm-checkin` (the GPS caption em-dashes although the op carries a real fix in `payload['gps']`). Nothing is fabricated and nothing is double-written, but on a money screen "what you see is not what confirm sends" deserves a ruling. Everything needed is already in the op's own durable payload, so the fix is additive and small. **(F2) a 4xx dead-letter strands the user IN-SESSION on `st-receive` / `pm-checkin` (pre-existing).** Those two keep `_opId` after a 4xx, so every later tap re-drains an op the drain will skip — stuck on FAILED with no way to make a new attempt, and on `st-receive` adjusting the counts does not clear `_opId` either. `pm-notes`/`pm-checklist` escape by editing; **`field-progress` does NOT escape in-session — that sentence was WRONG and is corrected here in round 4.** Its pre-mint queue check is never REACHED on a retry. After a 4xx on period p1, `_resolve` retains `_opId` (only `sent` clears it, `field_progress_screen.dart:399`) and `_pendingPeriodId` still names p1, so the next tap on p1 reads the slot at `:326-328` with `retryThisPeriod == true`, takes the re-drain branch at `:350`, and never gets to the queue check at `:364`. `QueueDrainProcessor` skips `failed` ops (`sync_processor.dart:182-187`), so the report comes back empty and `resolveDeliverState` maps straight back to `failed` — the foreman is stuck exactly as on `st-receive` and `pm-checkin`. `field-progress` escapes only ACROSS A RESTART (where `_adoptQueued` at `:216` correctly declines the dead-letter, so the relaunch is an honest fresh attempt) or by touching a DIFFERENT period, which is the case the queue check actually covers. So F2 is **three of the five screens in-session, not two**, and the ruling below has to cover `field-progress` too — the sentence made one of the five look already-handled when it is not. B-330 deliberately does NOT adopt dead-letters across a restart, so a relaunch is an honest fresh attempt — which currently makes restarting the app the only way out. Defensible, but it should be a decision. **ROUND 2 — the gate-4.5 FAIL: the ADOPTION WINDOW re-opened the same double-post on 4 of the 5 screens, with the network HEALTHY.** `_resumeQueued` is fired `unawaited` from `initState` and the adoption lands only AFTER `await repo.drain()` — one real HTTP round trip, and `app_services.dart:104` builds Dio with **no `connectTimeout`**, so nothing bounds it but the OS default. Throughout that window the screen is fully rendered, the CTA is live (`busy` is only `submitting`), `_opId` is still null and no queued card is shown. A tap took the mint branch and produced a **second key**; the nested `drain()` hit the processor's re-entrancy guard (`sync_processor.dart:175`) and returned an EMPTY report, so the screen even showed a reassuring `queued` — while the OUTER drain walked the FIFO and sent **both**. Reproduced deterministically with no offline state and no app kill in session 2: the server received **2 POST /gr under two distinct keys** = two goods receipts and two journal vouchers, exactly what `gr_idempotency_uq` (a partial unique index on the key ALONE) is supposed to allow. **THE FIX:** the check `field_progress._deliver` already had — ask the QUEUE before minting — now also in `st_receive._onConfirm`, `pm_checkin._onPrimary`, `pm_checklist._onSave` and `pm_notes._onSave`; each handler additionally flips its busy state SYNCHRONOUSLY before the queue read, so a second tap cannot slip into the gap the read itself opens. On the two FORM screens the check is SKIPPED once the payload has been edited (`_edited`, set exactly where `_opId` is dropped — plus `_seeding` on pm-notes so the read's own controller notifications are not mistaken for typing): a null `_opId` means BOTH "nothing of mine is queued" AND "the user just rewrote the body", and adopting in the second case would re-drain the OLD content, which is input loss and worse than the duplicate. **`st-receive` deliberately gains no such flag: a count change must not become a second GR while option (ข) is unruled.** The comments asserting that `_opId == null` means "nothing of mine is queued" (`st_receive_screen.dart:208`/`:293` and the same wording on the other three) were false for the whole window and are corrected in place; the shared helper header now also warns that a future `markInFlight` caller would hide an op from BOTH the drain and this matcher (`sync_queue.dart:28` excludes `inFlight` from `pending()`; there is no production caller today, so it is unreachable rather than latent). **VERIFICATION (round 2):** `flutter analyze` clean · `flutter test` **837** (828 + 9). Revert probes, ONE guard at a time, full suite each, reported per screen: st-receive pre-mint check → **1 red** (its own window test, MONEY) · pm-checkin → **1 red** · pm-notes → **1 red** · pm-checklist → **1 red** · pm-notes read-before-adopt ordering (`await loading` → `unawaited`) → **1 red** · pm-notes `_edited` → **2 red** · pm-checklist `_edited` → **1 red** · pm-notes `_seeding` → **0 red. THIS NUMBER WAS FALSE and is corrected here in round 3** (measured: removing `if (!_seeding)` left the suite GREEN at 837). Every OTHER red above is the test written for THAT guard, but the `_seeding` probe was reported without being run to a red, and the round-2 suite could not have produced one: every pm-notes fixture returned a work order with NO stored log, so `_seed` wrote `''` into already-empty controllers and notified nobody. The guard was real and UNPROVEN. Round 3 proves it. The window tests assert what the SERVER received, not what the screen believes. **The `pm_notes` ordering guard was genuinely unproven before this round** (reverting it left the suite green at 828) and is now proven — but only after the fixture was strengthened: the first attempt stayed GREEN both ways because `TextEditingController.text = ''` on an already-empty controller assigns an EQUAL `TextEditingValue` and notifies nobody, so a work order with no stored log can never fire the listener at all. The test now seeds a REAL stored body with the read 120 ms behind the drain. B-327's known flake (`sync_resume_drain_test.dart`) fired once during probing and once in 5 clean-tree runs — pre-existing, unrelated to this change, caused by no probe. **`field-progress` has NO window case, and the suite now says why:** its reads are serialized behind the drain (`_resumeQueued` = `await drain(); await _loadThenAdopt();`), so nothing is tappable until the drain returns. That is a liveness cost, not a money one, and the screen is left alone this round; if the reads are ever moved off the drain's critical path that expectation flips and the window case has to be written. **F1 IS AMENDED — it is not only a display gap, it is INPUT LOSS in one case. Answered by probe, not by reasoning:** **(A)** type inside the window, save AFTER the adoption lands → the queue ends `[k1]` carrying the OLD body while the screen still shows the NEW text (`ops=1 ids=[k1] causes=[OLD body] onScreen=NEW body`). **Round 2 does NOT close this** — the probe is byte-identical before and after the fix, because the save takes the tracked-`_opId` branch, which the pre-mint check never reaches. The direction for a future round is to let a set `_edited` override the tracked `_opId` in `_onSave`, which then ALSO requires clearing `_edited` at the mint or every subsequent tap would re-mint. **(B)** type inside the window, save INSIDE it → the pre-mint check ALONE regressed this from `[k1,k2]` `[OLD,NEW]` (duplicate write, input preserved) to `[k1]` `[OLD]` (one write, input lost); `_seeding` restores `[k1,k2]` `[OLD,NEW]` and a test now holds it. **(C)** `st-receive`: change a count inside the window, then confirm → `[k1]` carrying the ORIGINAL counts. The changed count is discarded and NO second receipt is created — deliberate, because a second GR against one PO is precisely option (ข) and Wei's to rule. **(D)** `pm-checklist` has no (B) gap: `_setResult` sets `_edited` unconditionally. **ROUND 3 — the last screen without the companion guard, the guard with no test, and a comment on the wrong declaration.** **(1) `field_progress._deliver` was the ONE handler that never flipped busy synchronously.** On dev its mint path held no `await` at all (guard → `_newOpId()` → `setState(sending)`), so `if (_state == sending) return;` was sufficient; round 1 inserted `await repo.due()` IN FRONT of that guard's own precondition — and field-progress is the screen the pre-mint pattern was copied FROM, so it is the one that did not also get round 2's synchronous flip. Two taps inside that read produce TWO ops on one period. Offline that is two keys; ONLINE the server takes the delivery exactly once (the C3 guard at `subcon.ts:758` admits only a `pending` period and 409s the replay), which parks a permanent 4xx dead-letter every future drain skips — B-330's own F2 — while the screen paints the failed bar over a delivery that SUCCEEDED. Not money, but a user-visible regression on a path that was safe on dev. **THE FIX:** `_state` AND `_pendingPeriodId` are flipped before ANY await, and the tracked slot is read BEFORE that flip — hoisting the `_pendingPeriodId` write above the slot read would make `_pendingPeriodId == period.id` trivially true and turn a tap on period A into a re-drain of period B's op (probed: 2 red). The two branch-local `setState(sending)` restatements are dropped, so the flip is the single place that sets it. **(2) `_seeding` had a claimed probe and no test.** Proven now with the ORDINARY case the round-2 fixtures could not express: a work order that ALREADY CARRIES a log. Read it, kill the app, relaunch INSIDE the adoption window with the replay held, tap save without typing. With the guard the queued op is adopted → one write carrying what the technician typed. Without it the seed sets `_edited`, the pre-mint check is skipped, and the minted second op carries THE TEXT THE READ JUST PUT THERE — and FIFO replays it LAST, so the stale stored log overwrites the real one. That is input loss, not a duplicate. **(3) A doc comment had migrated onto the wrong declaration in the MONEY file.** `grPoIdField` was inserted INSIDE `buildReceiptPayload`'s doc comment (`st_receive_agg.dart`), so the whole `Build the POST /gr body …` block — every load-bearing omission, `price`, `name`, `no`, `qty_rejected` — documented `const String grPoIdField = 'po_id';`, and the builder kept only the `[idempotencyKey]` paragraph. The analyzer is silent and dartdoc renders it wrong. Moved back; same class as the round-1 comment that asserted the opposite of the code. **(4) FIXTURE AUDIT — every fixture in both new test files, asked what condition it must create and whether it CAN. Three defects, two of them in fixtures written THIS round.** **(a)** the first `_SlowDueProgressRepo` delayed and THEN read the queue, so the second tap's read observed the first tap's enqueue and the pre-mint check deduped the pair by itself — the revert probe left the suite GREEN, which is how it was caught. Snapshot-then-delay is both the fix and the faithful ordering: on one drift connection the second SELECT is queued behind the first SELECT but AHEAD of the INSERT, because the first tap cannot issue that INSERT until its own SELECT returns. **(b)** `_Transport.statusByEndpoint` was declared with a doc naming tests that do not exist — an unused capability asserting a user it does not have; removed. **(c)** `pending_op_adoption_test`'s "adopts the OLDEST match" could not distinguish oldest from first: `findAdoptableOpAmong` does NO sorting, it walks the list it is handed, so the two `createdAt` values were decoration and swapping them changes nothing. Reworded to what it measures (FIRST match in the given order), and the oldest-first property is now exercised where it actually lives — over the REAL queue, enqueued newest-first, so `pending()`'s sort is the only thing that can make it pass. Everything else checked out: `_NotesRepo` genuinely creates the SILENT-seed condition (it just cannot create the noisy one — that is `_StoredNotesRepo`); `_SlowNotesRepo` still holds the read-before-adopt ordering after being re-parented onto it (re-probed, 1 red); `_ProgressRepo`'s two pending periods, `_ChecklistRepo`'s one line, `_FixedGps`, `_kill`, the five sidecars and the dict are each load-bearing in at least one red; `_unusedDio` is called by nothing, and any un-stubbed read would throw on a Dio with no baseUrl. **VERIFICATION (round 3):** `flutter analyze` clean · `flutter test` **841** (837 + 4: two field-progress double-tap widget tests, the pm-notes noisy-seed widget test, the real-queue ordering unit test) · 3 consecutive clean full-suite runs, plus 5 more before the last two probes. **Revert probes, ONE at a time, full suite each:** field-progress synchronous flip removed → **1 red** (`Expected: <1> Actual: <2>` — two ops on one period) · `if (!_seeding)` → unconditional `_edited = true` → **1 red** (the second key is sitting in the queue) · the OVER-fix, hoisting `_pendingPeriodId` above the slot read → **2 red** (the new different-periods control + the existing period-B ownership test) · `InMemorySyncQueue.pending()`'s `createdAt` sort removed → **3 red** (the new real-queue test + 2 pre-existing) · pm-notes `await loading` → `unawaited` re-run after the fixture re-parent → **1 red**. **REPORTED GREEN, not adjusted:** reverting the flip leaves the different-periods test GREEN — it is a negative control against the over-fix, and only the hoist probe gives it teeth. **B-327's flake — CORRECTED, AND NOT WAVED THROUGH.** Measured over the whole round: `sync_resume_drain_test.dart` fired in **4 of 16** full-suite runs on this tree and **0 of 7** on the pinned round-2 tree, the last 4 of those interleaved with this tree's runs on the same loaded machine. Root cause located rather than assumed: the failing assertion is `expect(await queue.length(), 0)` at `sync_resume_drain_test.dart:291` — the `adapter.replayed` assertion immediately above it PASSES, so the replay did happen and only the `markSynced` had not been observed inside the test's fixed `Future.delayed(50ms)` REAL-time budget inside `runAsync`. It is a wall-clock budget, so anything that lengthens the run exposes it, and round 3 lengthens the `offline_restart` file. Nothing here touches pm-checkin, the resume trigger or drain timing, and it passes in isolation every time — but **0/7 vs 4/16 means this round cannot claim to be neutral on that rate, and it is not claimed.** The fix belongs to B-327: poll until the queue drains instead of sleeping a fixed 50 ms. Deliberately NOT done here — it is outside this round's three findings and would be an unreviewed edit to an unrelated test. **STILL OPEN AFTER ROUND 3, DISCLOSED RATHER THAN CLOSED:** the other four screens' synchronous flips have no double-tap test of their own (each needs its own snapshot-then-delay `due()` fixture) — **CLOSED in round 4 below**; F1 case (A) is untouched and is Wei's option (ง); the `st-receive` two-partial-receipts question is untouched and is option (ข); the field-progress read-serialization tripwire is untouched. **ROUND 4 — the four flips that had no mechanical protection, and an F2 sentence that was wrong.** **(1) The four synchronous flips round 2 added were asserted by READING the handlers, never by testing them.** Round 3 said so and left it as a fast-follow; gate 4.5 then wrote the missing fixture instead of accepting the hand-check, and measured what the disclosure had only asserted: all four flips are correct as written, but **removing any one of them left the suite at 841 with nothing red** — `st-receive`, the MONEY screen, had **zero** mechanical protection on its most recently-fixed invariant. The concrete regression that would ship: a later round tidies `_onConfirm` by moving the flip below the `findAdoptableOp` await — **exactly the shape `field_progress` had on dev** — the count stays 841 and gate 4.5 has nothing to catch it. This is the third consecutive round in which this class appeared on this branch, which is why it was closed now rather than deferred again. **THE FIX (test-only — no `lib/` change, no behaviour change):** four widget tests in `offline_restart_adoption_test.dart` group 1e, one per screen, each driving its OWN snapshot-then-delay `due()` fixture (`_SlowDueRecvRepo` / `_SlowDueCheckinRepo` / `_SlowDueChecklistRepo` / `_SlowDueNotesRepo`) over a shared `_slowDue` helper that states the ordering rule ONCE. Each asserts what is in the QUEUE and then, with the signal restored, what the SERVER received. **THE FIXTURE TRAP, stated because it is the whole reason the pm-notes test is not decorative:** the natural way to give pm-notes a realistic body is to type one — and typing sets `_edited`, which SKIPS the pre-mint queue read and sends the handler down a mint path with **no await between the guard and `_newOpId()`**, so the second tap would find `_opId` already set, re-drain instead of minting, and the revert probe would come back **GREEN** with the flip gone. Parenting the fixture on `_StoredNotesRepo` puts a real body in via the SEED, which `_seeding` correctly does not count as typing, so `_edited` stays false and the tap goes through the read the test is about. Same class as round 3's delay-then-snapshot defect, one level up; the test asserts the seed actually landed rather than assuming it. **NO NEGATIVE CONTROL WAS ADDED FOR THESE FOUR, and the reason is stated rather than left as an omission:** over-fixing would mean a flip that swallows a genuinely SEPARATE write, and each of these four screens carries ONE anchor for its whole mount — the only genuinely separate write is a LATER one, which is already held by the `boundaries` test (the key is released on sync, so the next receipt for the same PO gets its own) and by the `an edit mints instead of adopting` group. `field-progress` needed its own control because it is the one screen with N anchors live at once. **VERIFICATION (round 4):** `flutter analyze` clean · `flutter test` **845** (841 + 4). **Revert probes, ONE flip at a time, full suite each, reported separately:** `st_receive._onConfirm` flip removed → **1 red** (`Expected: <1> Actual: <2>` — two POST /gr under two keys = two goods receipts + two journal vouchers) · `pm_checkin._onPrimary` → **1 red** (`<1>` vs `<2>` — two check-ins on one work order) · `pm_checklist._onSave` → **1 red** (`<1>` vs `<2>`) · `pm_notes._onSave` → **1 red** (`<1>` vs `<2>` — two close-outs). In each probe the red was that screen's own new test and **no other screen's test moved**, which is the point of four fixtures rather than one. Each screen file was restored and confirmed byte-identical (`git diff` empty) before the next probe. **B-327's flake** fired in **1 of the 6** full-suite runs of this round and the immediate re-run on the identical tree was clean; still untouched, still its own id — fixing an unrelated test file at this stage is how this branch acquired two of its defects. **(2) The F2 disclosure was wrong about `field-progress` and is corrected in F2 above** — it does not escape in-session; its queue check is never reached on a retry, so F2 is three screens, not two. **MERGE NOTE (recorded, not acted on):** `git merge-tree --write-tree dev HEAD` reports a content conflict in this file — both sides appended at the tail, dev gained B-331..B-334 after the branch point. The resolution is to KEEP ALL FIVE rows; the branch was deliberately not rebased. **ROUND 5 (B-341) — F1 case (A) is CLOSED; F2 is NOT, and was measured rather than assumed.** Wei's B-341 ruling records that quietening the CTA would resolve both. It resolves neither on its own, and both were probed rather than reasoned about. **F1 (A):** the probe types inside the window and saves after it closes; on a CTA-quiet-only build the server received `causes=[OLD body]` with the NEW body on screen — the reported shape, reproduced. The save is AFTER the window, so no amount of quiet reaches it. Closed instead by extending the rule `_onSave` has applied since round 2 — do not adopt an op whose payload the user has since replaced — to the OTHER place that adopts, `_resumeQueued`'s adoption on `pm_notes` and `pm_checklist`, checked AFTER the queue read. Probe: drop it and F1 (A) goes red on its screen (`pm_notes` 2 red, `pm_checklist` 1 red). Cases (B), (C) and (D) are unchanged — (C) in particular is deliberately untouched, since a changed count becoming a second GR is option (ข) and Wei's to rule. **F2:** NOT closed. `_settling` is long false by the time a write can fail and the failed branch never consults it. The six-screen matrix was run against this branch's `lib/` AND against dev's, and answers identically: STRANDED in-session = `st_receive`, `pm_checkin`, `field_progress` (three, confirming round 4's correction); ESCAPES = `pm_notes`/`pm_checklist` through an EDIT only, `field_stock` on a plain re-tap. It is now six tests rather than a paragraph, so the next round cannot re-derive it by reading. F2's options below stand. | (ก) ratify B-330 as applied — client-only, no sacred file, no contract or payload change, and every behaviour change is in the direction of sending FEWER writes (recommended) · (ข) different ownership rule for `st-receive` — e.g. anchor on PO + line-set rather than PO alone, if two partial receipts against one PO must be able to sit in the queue simultaneously (today the second would adopt the first's key while it is still pending) · (ค) revert and accept the duplicate. **Separately, for F1 (now display AND input loss — see case (A) in ROUND 2):** (ก) restore the display from the queued payload on all four · (ข) `st-receive` only · (ค) leave it, the `queued` card already warns · (ง) **new after round 2:** let an edit made inside the adoption window override the adopted id, so a typed body is never silently replaced by the queued one — client-only and small, but it changes what the `queued` card asserts, so it should be ruled rather than assumed. **For F2:** (ก) clear `_opId` on a `permanentlyFailed` outcome so the next tap is a genuinely new write (matches what the restart already does) · (ข) surface the dead-letter with an explicit retry affordance (needs copy → an i18n round) · (ค) leave as is | | รอ Wei ตอบ — ไม่ block งาน |

| B-335 | `attendance` has **NO correction path** — the second INSERT everyone calls "the correction" ADDS to the day instead of replacing it, so REDUCING a man's day RAISES his pay | Found by B-332's gate-4.5 review, in the load-bearing schema comment that justified refusing `unique(worker_id, day)`: *"a second INSERT is the ONLY way to correct a day"* (`packages/db/src/schema/finance.ts`). **The INSERT is accepted. It does not correct.** `createLaborPayroll` pays by SUMMING attendance rows, so the "correction" is added to the original. **Proven live on a 500/day worker**: one `full` day → payroll `500`; filing the `half` correction → payroll `750`. A day genuinely corrected to half owes `250`, so a supervisor reducing the day moved the pay `500 → 750`, the wrong way. **There is no correction path at all**: no `UPDATE`/`PUT`/`PATCH`/`DELETE` on attendance anywhere in `registerLaborRoute`, and no supersede/void/superseded_by column on the table. The only way to change a recorded day today is direct SQL. Wei ruled the third way: **disable the CTA until the queue read completes**, so neither the silent drop nor the duplicate can happen. This deviates from the prototype's behaviour, which is why it needed a ruling rather than an implementer's judgement — the prototype has no offline state at all. Applies to `st_receive`, `pm_checkin`, `pm_checklist`, `pm_notes`, `field_progress` and `field_stock` **as one change**, because the same shape sits in all six (`st_receive_screen.dart:323-332` is byte-identical to `field_stock_screen.dart:508-517`). It also resolves **F1 case A** (an edit typed inside the window and saved after it closes was discarded — with the CTA quiet there is no such save) and **F2** (a 4xx dead-letter stranding the user in-session on three of the five screens, not two as the row first claimed). **APPLIED — `feature/b341-cta-quiet` (mobile only: 7 files in `apps/mobile/lib` + 1 test file; no sacred file, no i18n key, no contract change).** **WHAT "UNTIL THE QUEUE SETTLES" WAS SETTLED TO MEAN, and why:** the window is the screen's own QUEUE READ — `repo.due()` + `findAdoptableOp` — and **the drain is no longer in it**. `_resumeQueued` used to be `await drain(); then adopt`; it is now `adopt; then await drain()`. Two things make that safe and make the window BOUNDED, which the ruling requires because a CTA quiet forever is worse than the defect it fixes. **(i) THE SHRINK LEMMA.** A drain can only ever REMOVE ops from the adoptable set for one identity: `markSynced` deletes, `markFailed` makes an op non-adoptable (only `pending` is adoptable), and a 5xx/transport deferral re-enqueues the SAME id still `pending` (`sync_processor.dart` `_deferPending`). Nothing in a drain ADDS one. So reading the queue BEFORE the drain returns a SUPERSET of reading it after — adopt-first cannot miss anything adopt-after would have found, and waiting for the drain only delayed an answer the durable queue already had. **(ii) THE BOUND IS STRUCTURAL, NOT A CHOSEN DURATION.** With the drain out of it the window contains **no network call at all**: `SyncQueue.pending()`'s only two implementations are an in-memory list and the local drift/SQLite store, while the drain goes out over the Dio built at `app_services.dart:104` **with no `connectTimeout`** — the genuinely unbounded wait. No timeout constant was invented, because none would be honest: a store so wedged that a local read never returns would freeze the MINT path identically (`_onConfirm` awaits the same `due()` before it mints), so releasing the CTA on a timer would buy a spinner instead of a greyed button and nothing else. What IS handled is a read that THROWS — every screen releases in a `finally`, so an unanswerable question opens the button rather than parking it, and the pre-mint check is what holds from there (proven: group `a queue read that throws opens the CTA, and the mint site holds`, 6 screens, each asserting what the SERVER received). **THE DISPLAY COST, paid back:** adopting before the drain means a queued card can outlive its subject by one round trip, so each screen re-asks once the drain returns (`_reconcile`) and takes the card down if the write is no longer outstanding — landing on exactly the state the old drain-first ordering reached. It is skipped the moment the user acts (guarded on the adopted id AND the queued state), so it can never clobber a live flow. **THE SIX SCREENS ARE NOT ONE SHAPE, and were not forced into one.** `st_receive`/`pm_checkin` anchor on a widget parameter, so their window is the local read ALONE. `pm_checklist` also anchors on a parameter and its load runs in parallel. `pm_notes` must await its READ before the queue read — `_load` seeds three controllers, which fires `_onEdited`, which drops `_opId` — and that costs the user nothing, because the form and its CTA are not rendered until `_loaded`. `field_stock` must await its read too (the warehouse is resolved BY the load, not passed in) and it was already CTA-dead for that stretch (`_canSubmit` is false over an unloaded shelf). `field_progress` differs most: N anchors live at once, resolved by an async read, and the foreman can swap the whole set without leaving the screen — so the flag is re-raised in `_selectContract`, not just at mount, and its drain was moved off the read's critical path (it used to blank the entire period list behind an unbounded replay). **WHAT THE USER SEES WHILE IT IS QUIET — and the MINT LIST IS EMPTY.** No new copy was needed and none was invented: four screens (`st_receive`, `pm_checkin`, `pm_checklist`, `pm_notes`) render the CTA in the SAME muted-fill + spinner they already use for a submit; the two that have no spinner in that bar (`field_progress` renders one button per row, where N spinners would be noise; `field_stock`'s bar has none at all) render the SAME muted fill they already use for "not actionable". Both are existing visual vocabulary, and the state is honest — the screen really is working, on a local read. **F1 CASE A — NOT closed by the quiet CTA. Measured, not reasoned.** The probe types inside the window and saves AFTER it closes: on a CTA-quiet-only build the server received `causes=['สายพานขาด']` (the OLD body) while the screen showed the NEW one — **red**, exactly the reported shape. The quiet CTA cannot close it, because the save is after the window, not in it: the adoption lands on top of the edit and the next save takes the `tracked != null` branch. What closes it is the rule the MINT SITE ALREADY APPLIES, applied at the other place that adopts — `_edited` (the body/results on screen are no longer what the queued op carries, so that op is not this write). One condition on `pm_notes` and `pm_checklist`'s adoption guard, checked AFTER the read. Not new policy: `_onSave` has refused to adopt after an edit since round 2, and this is the same sentence in the second place it was missing. `st_receive` deliberately gains NO such flag — a changed count must not become a second GR while option (ข) is unruled — so its F1 case (C) is untouched. **F2 — NOT closed, and quietening the CTA does not touch it.** Measured rather than assumed: the six-screen dead-letter matrix was run against THIS branch's `lib/` and against **dev's `lib/`** (stash the seven files, run the same probe) and it answers **identically**, because `_settling` is long false by the time a write can fail and the failed branch never consults it. The matrix, now recorded as tests: **STRANDED in-session** = `st_receive`, `pm_checkin`, `field_progress` (three, as the round-4 correction said — `field_progress`'s re-tap takes the re-drain branch and never reaches the queue check). **ESCAPES** = `pm_notes` and `pm_checklist` **only through an edit**, and `field_stock` on a plain re-tap (its `_resolve` releases `_opId` on BOTH terminal outcomes). F2 therefore stays open and still wants its own ruling — the options on this row are unchanged. **VERIFICATION:** `flutter analyze` clean · `flutter test` **954** (baseline 933; +21) · zero Thai codepoints in the seven touched `lib` files (grepped directly — the i18n hook does not fire in a worktree, B-243). **REVERT PROBE, per screen, whole file each, because one red is not proof six are covered:** `st_receive` quiet term removed → **1 red** (its own case) · `pm_checkin` → **1 red** · `pm_notes` → **2 red** (its case + F1 case A) · `pm_checklist` → **2 red** (its case + F1 case A) · `field_progress` → **1 red** · `field_stock` → **1 red**. In every probe the red was that screen's own test and no other screen's test moved. **Three further probes on the other halves:** put the drain back in FRONT of the queue read (`st_receive`) → **3 red** (its window case, its unbounded-drain case, its throwing-read case) · drop `_edited` from `pm_notes`' adoption → **2 red** · from `pm_checklist`' adoption → **1 red** · disable `_reconcile` on `st_receive` → **1 red**. **FIXTURE AUDIT — every fixture asked what condition it must create and whether it CAN.** The window fixtures hold `due()` open, not the transport, because after this change the window IS the queue read; they reuse round 3's `_slowDue` helper, which SNAPSHOTS at the call and delays after — delay-then-read would let a second tap observe the first tap's enqueue, so the pre-mint check would dedupe the pair by itself and the revert probe would come back GREEN (measured once on this branch already, and the reason that helper carries a paragraph). The window tests additionally HOLD THE REPLAY OPEN: without the gate the drain resolves k1 before the read snapshots it, there is no outstanding op, and every assertion passes over an empty queue — caught by writing them ungated first and seeing `Actual: []`. `_tapCtaSpinner` asserts the spinner is actually there before tapping it, so a test can never pass because the CTA was merely un-findable, and it taps INSIDE the CTA's own `HitTestBehavior.opaque` GestureDetector so the tap is really delivered at the button. `_expectMutedCta` reads the rendered fill rather than trusting a label's absence, since the two greying screens keep their label. The new `_ThrowingDue*` fixtures flip back to a working read before the user's tap, or the mint site's own read would fail too and the test would prove nothing about the pre-mint check. **One defect found, in a fixture-driven assertion of my own `lib` code:** field-stock's reconcile captured the load's default project BEFORE `await _future`, i.e. before the load had set it, so the "restore" would have blanked the slot to an em-dash instead of returning it to the primary project — caught only because the test picked the SECOND project, which is the only way the default and the charged value differ. Fixed and held by that test. | (ก เงียบปุ่มจนกว่าคิวจะนิ่ง) | 🟢 RULED 2026-08-08 · **APPLIED `feature/b341-cta-quiet`** · mobile · one round across all six screens · prototype-deviation recorded here rather than in six places · **F1 case A closed by probe; F2 measured NOT closed and still open** · **ROUND 2 — the gate FAILED the first attempt on a CRITICAL MONEY defect the reorder itself created, and the FAIL was right.** The reorder is sound (a drain only ever SHRINKS what is adoptable, so reading first returns a superset — every queue mutation enumerated: `markSynced` removes, `markFailed` makes unadoptable, `_deferPending` re-enqueues the SAME id). What was wrong is the COMPENSATION it required. **(1)** `_reconcile` re-asked `findAdoptableOp` and read a null as "gone → `idle`". Null is BOTH answers — a synced op is REMOVED and a 4xx dead-letter is UNADOPTABLE — so a 201 and a permanent rejection were reported identically, and reported as `idle`, which means *nothing enqueued*. On a receipt the server had ACCEPTED, the screen therefore stated the write never happened, dropped `_opId`, and handed the next tap a clean slate over an empty queue. Measured on `POST /gr`: branch `acceptedKeys = [k…1170491951, k…263588553]` — two GR, two JV — against dev's one. It now resolves through the screen's OWN `_resolve` fed the drain's own `DrainReport`, the identical path a manual retry takes, which already separates the three outcomes by construction (the report when it touched the op; else that op's ID in the queue — gone = synced, `failed` = dead-letter, `pending` = still due). **(2)** `_stillShowing` meant *"the user has not acted"* and could not mean it: the documented manual retry lands on `_opId == adopted && _state == queued` whenever the write is still due, bit-for-bit the tuple an untouched adoption leaves. Replaced by a `_reconcileArmed` LATCH — raised at adoption, lowered at every user entry point, never raised again — because `_state` is cyclic and this fact must be monotonic. Without it the reconciliation is a SECOND writer racing a flow already in progress over the same terminal side effects: st-receive's `confirmed` POPS, so an unprompted second one takes st-grlist off the stack with it. **THE OPEN QUESTION, ANSWERED:** a succeeded adopted op shows the screen's own SUCCESS state, never `idle` — and on st-receive that includes the pop back to st-grlist, because B-266 left the pop as the confirmation. **pm-checkin's caption** is now seeded from the adopted op's `createdAt` (the same instant `_acquireAndSubmit` stores in `_submitTime`), not the `DateTime.now()` fallback, which captioned a check-in made at 08:03 with the moment the signal returned. **The shared header's claim** *"the window contains no network call at all"* was FALSE for pm_notes / field_stock / field_progress, which each await their own read chain first; corrected, together with what actually bounds those three (there is no live CTA to take away yet). **Evidence:** flutter analyze clean · test 954 → **979**; all 25 new tests RED on the pre-fix `78afe12` (0 pass / 25 fail — none passes on the defect for another reason); latch revert probe per screen, each red alone — st-receive 1 · pm-checkin 1 · pm-notes 1 · pm-checklist 1 · field-progress 2 · field-stock 2. Residual filed as **B-344**. |

| B-358 | **the apps/web test environment has no DOM, so no JSX seam can ever be pinned there — and the pad→request wiring is UNPINNED because of it** — surfaced by the B-331 gate-4.5 (F1), re-scoped by its second round | `apps/web/vitest.config.ts` sets `environment: "node"`, and the workspace has **no `jsdom`, no `happy-dom` and no `@testing-library/react`** (`require.resolve` fails for all three). The consequence is not cosmetic: the gate mutated `<SignaturePad onChange={onChange} />` to `onChange={() => onChange(null)}` — every stroke discarded, the close still firing — and **1776 tests stayed green**. **THE FIRST ANSWER OVERCLAIMED, and this row now carries the correction.** Round 1 removed the callback and said "the gate's mutation no longer type-checks". The gate demonstrated two edits, each `tsc --noEmit` exit 0 with 1794 green: **(A)** `capture={createSignatureCapture()}` and **(B)** `capture={{ ...capture.current }}`. (B) is the dangerous one and it is INVISIBLE: the shallow copy shares the `strokes` array, so the canvas renders the customer's signature perfectly and only `width`/`height` land on the copy; the real object keeps `width === 0`, `encodeSignatureInk` takes its degenerate-viewport branch, and the work order closes with `customer_sign` NULL while the technician watches the ink and reads a success toast. (Verbatim (A) and (B) against the new prop shape both FAIL to compile — the `capture` prop is gone — which is a shape change, not a defence; the analogues above are the honest re-run. The analogue of (B) also compiles clean and green, and that is now the CORRECT outcome: it is harmless, proven by the test above rather than argued.) So: **the web pad→request wiring is unpinned pending a DOM test environment**, with both probes recorded here as its reproduction. Every other component whose behaviour lives in an event handler is in the same position, and the next one may not be restructurable. | B-366 | **web `pm` close modal — the confirm button is no longer ALWAYS enabled, which is a visible departure from `pototype/pm3.jsx` L128-147** (raised by the B-331 gate-4.5, F3; the fix is shipped, the ratification is what is asked for) | **The hole it closes.** Web left confirm permanently enabled and sent `readSignatureCapture(...) ?? undefined`, so ink the encoder REFUSES was silently discarded and the close still reported success. Client-supplied values Postgres refuses now answer **400**; server-computed overflows keep their **500**. The rule: remap ⟺ **every** parameter that could have overflowed is a value the client sent (body ∪ path ∪ query) — `every`, not `any`, so over-including a candidate can only KEEP a 500, and unattributable always means 500. Fails **closed**: a non-English or reworded PG `detail` yields no `10^N`, no candidates, 500. Proven not a cover-up: `approve-payment` overflows `ap_billing.amount` from a server-computed gross on an **empty body** — same SQLSTATE, same handler, same path as the 400 — and still 500s; a genuine broken query (42P01) still 500s on the very route that 400s for a bad uuid. Codes: 22003, 22P02, 22007/22008 in; 22001 out (**0 `varchar(n)` in the whole schema** — verified in both the drizzle source and the SQL), 22012/22023 out. **TWO RESIDUALS, pinned by tests rather than argued away:** (1) a same-type collision — a payload carrying the literal string `"undefined"` turns a `String(undefined)` server bug into a 400, and type-aware matching CANNOT fix it because both sides are strings; (2) the `every` quantifier means a genuine client overflow sharing a statement with a server-derived value ≥ threshold keeps its 500, so that op still wedges. In both, **the ERROR-level log is the compensating control, not the status code** — the module says so in a section headed “WHAT THIS DOES NOT GUARANTEE”. Closing (1) needs a different mechanism than value matching → **รอ Wei**. |

That is B-084's own consequence and precedent-consistent, so it shipped; but if the answer is "proc too", **the correct fix is the seed perms matrix, NOT a looser handler gate.** Also note **403 now precedes 404** on `PUT /vendors/:id`, so an unprivileged caller no longer gets an existence oracle on a supplier id. Test quality proven by two mutants: removing the check → 34/34 becomes **5 red**; *moving the gate below `db.update`* still answers 403 but kills the money assertion (`expected 'BAY 999-0-00000-1' to be 'KBANK 012-3-45678-9'`) — so the write-absence assertions are load-bearing, not ornamental. |
| B-396 | api `gl` — **`POST /gl/jv` writes a balanced JV into the ledger with no permission gate** (the second door B-394 does not close) | Raised by gate-4.5 while reviewing B-394 and then **confirmed live**: `POST /api/v1/gl/jv` as the seeded `wh` role (zero finance perms) answers **400**, not 403 — i.e. it reaches the handler's validation, so no authz gate exists. `gl.ts:1340-1345` → `createJv` (`:274+`) validates balance, tenant ownership and period-lock, then inserts `jvs` + `jvLines` in one tx (`:376-392`) with `loadCaller`/`permAllowed` never called; the only gated doors in that file are `/gl/close-period` (`:1347`) and `/gl/post` (`:1368`). **Why it matters now:** B-394 just denied the `exec` and `wh` roles at `/gl/revrec/:id/post`, and they can still hand-post an equivalent balanced JV here. Exactly the "two doors to one effect" shape B-377 was raised on — so the claim "money posting is gated" should not be treated as complete until this is answered. | (ก) gate on **`finance.approve`** — a manual JV *is* a posting, matching `/gl/post` and B-394 · (ข) gate on **`finance.create`** — a manual JV is an operational document and posting is the separate `/gl/post` step · (ค) Wei confirms intended — manual JV entry is open to any tenant member | **ก** (2026-08-14 · Wei, AskUserQuestion) — gated on `finance.approve`. **The stake Wei ruled on, stated before the ask:** `sale` (Sales/REM) holds finance.create but NOT approve, so (ข) would have kept a salesperson able to write ledger entries directly. New evidence presented with the question: the `jv` table carries no status and no posted flag (id · company_id · no · source_doc · period_id · memo · timestamps) and `/gl/post` writes its OWN jv rows rather than promoting a draft — so (ข)'s rationale ("posting is the separate /gl/post step") was factually void. **Recorded because gate-4.5 FAILED an earlier attempt that shipped (ก) with this cell still empty and a code comment attributing the options to Wei — the options were the blocker author's; refuting (ข)'s rationale is not Wei picking (ก).** | ✅ RESOLVED — merged. Gate mirrors `/gl/post` line-for-line (`loadCaller` → 403 "caller cannot be attributed", then `permAllowed(caller.perms, FINANCE_MODULE, "approve")`), placed after the 401 and BEFORE the body is read. **Newly denies 6 of 9 seeded roles** — pm · proc · site · sale · wh · exec; dir · acc · finmgr retain it. Proven LIVE in the deployed build: Warehouse went from **201 with jv count 7→8** to **403 with 7→7**, while a Director control got 400 (not 403), proving the gate does not blanket-deny. Test quality by mutation: gate deleted → 3 red, all `expected 201 to be 403`; a mutant that moves the guard BELOW the write still answers 403 with an identical body, so the `expect(inserted).toEqual([])` assertions are the load-bearing ones. No e2e regresses — all 5 write call sites use `finMgr` (suda@ = Finance Manager, holds approve); the 6th is an anon 401 probe. |
| B-397 | api `bank` — **`POST /bank/export-batch` re-emitted every approved transfer PV on EVERY call, so one cash instruction could land in two bank files** | Audit finding H4. The handler was a pure build-and-return that never mutated anything — `bank.ts:623-624` said so itself ("it does NOT persist the PVs (batch_id persistence / post-export lock is out of this task's scope)"). Eligibility was only `approved + transfer`, and `batchId` was minted then stored nowhere. Reproduced: two calls over one PV → 200 both times, the identical instruction line under two different batch_ids, zero writes captured. **Worse than a double-click hazard: no paid/exported state existed at all, so even one correct upload left the PV re-emitted in every future batch, forever.** | (ก) **[shipped]** implement the lock the spec already states — stamp `pv.batch_id` inside a transaction with a B-149 guard on the FINAL UPDATE's WHERE (`isNull(pvs.batchId)`), exclude stamped PVs from eligibility, emit ONLY the rows the UPDATE returned · (ข) leave it and gate at the UI | **ก** (2026-08-14 · orch-B — **no ruling needed, this is spec transcription**: the lock rule is stated three times and they agree — `pototype/bank.jsx:226` "ล็อกการเปลี่ยนแปลง PV ที่ส่งธนาคารแล้ว · ถ้าต้องแก้ ต้องสร้างใบใหม่", `pototype/real-forms2.jsx:463-471`, `docs/handoff/FUNCTIONS.md:88`; `bank.jsx:166` gives the waiting-vs-sent two-state filter; `batch_id` is a pv field per data-dictionary.html:90 + erd.html:85 and the column already existed) | ✅ RESOLVED — merged. **No migration, no new table, no contract change.** Proven LIVE on real Postgres (what the stub cannot do — it has no BEGIN/COMMIT): 1st export 200 with 3 pv_ids and **3 rows stamped in the DB**, 2nd export **409** "no payment voucher is waiting to be sent"; every probe write reverted and verified (0 rows differing from snapshot, 0 stray stamps). Test quality by mutation: removing the guard → 3 red incl. two emitted-FILE assertions; **the verifier's own nastier mutant** — leave pv_ids honest but build the instructions from the pre-read list — still dies on `content` not containing the unstamped PV, so the "if the stamp did not happen the instruction is not written" property is tested at file level, not by proxy. **A pre-existing guard caught a real defect in the first cut:** `list-order.enforce.test.ts` (B-323) flagged the emit comparator as tie-blind — it ranked rows by position in the candidate read, but that read is a bare `db.select(pvs)` with no ORDER BY, i.e. **the join plan's order wearing a map**; replaced with `newestFirst`, the house comparator this file already uses at :242/:609. **Web rode along (cross-zone, required not optional):** `buildExportBody` always sends `pv_ids`, so every web export is an explicit-ids call and one stale row would 409 the WHOLE batch and send nothing — `ExportPv` gained `batchId`, `toExportPv` reads it, `isExportEligible` excludes sent vouchers. **Residuals filed, not buried:** no batch ENTITY is persisted (only the stamp), so the prototype's "ประวัติ Export" card still has no wire source; `batch_id` has no defined semantics in the dictionary (uuid vs file name vs FK); the spec offers NO re-export affordance (only re-download), so a lost file today has no remedy but a new voucher; and a crash between COMMIT and the HTTP reply locks vouchers with the operator holding no file. → **B-399**. |
| B-398 | api `ap` — **`POST /ap/pv` let one `ap_billing` be covered by two PVs, so a vendor payable could be paid twice, exactly** | Audit finding H3. Reproduced: two POSTs with the same `billing_ids` both returned 201, and B-315's server-derived gross made the duplicate EXACT rather than smaller. The create path **never queried the pv table at all**, so no coverage guard could exist; the insert was not even in a transaction; pv's only index is the non-unique `pv_company_idx`; and there is **ZERO `update(apBillings)` in the whole API**, so `ap_billing.status` (free text, default draft) is never transitioned and was not usable as a marker. Distinct from B-149, whose guard at ap.ts correctly stops double-approving the SAME pv row but not two different rows over one billing. | (ก) **[Wei ruled · shipped]** one billing may be covered by at most one LIVE PV → 409; `billing_ids[]` stays plural so a PV may still pay several billings · (ข) allow several PVs per billing, guard the cumulative Σ ≤ billing gross (needs a partial-payment field the spec does not have) · (ค) narrow `billing_ids[]` to exactly one member (matches the prototype form but contradicts the shipped contract) | **ก** (2026-08-14 · Wei, AskUserQuestion) — **this is a RULING, not a spec transcription, and the code comments say so.** The spec never states the rule: `pototype/ap.jsx:161-164` gives each PV a single `ref` and `:246` one fixed Select, while `docs/handoff/api-contract.md:65` + `erd.html:85` use `billing_ids[]` plural; no seed row reuses an AP number but nothing declares that as a rule; `ap_billing` has no status column in the data dictionary at all. | ✅ RESOLVED — merged. **No migration, no new column, and no invented status value** — deliberately: the prototype `STATUS` map (`ds.jsx:84-92`) has no value meaning "on a PV", and `StatusBadge` falls back to "ร่าง" for an unknown one (`ds.jsx:94`), so writing an invented status would have put a wrong badge on a real screen. Instead createPv is wrapped in `db.transaction`; `tx.selectForUpdate(apBillings, …)` both proves tenant ownership and serializes concurrent creates; the coverage read runs INSIDE the tx AFTER the lock; clash → 409 naming the billing id. Proven LIVE: sequential 201 → **409** on the same billing, and **two simultaneous POSTs returned exactly [201, 409]** with one PV covering the billing afterwards — the row lock works against real processes, which no stub can show. Test quality by mutation (verifier ran 11): guard removed = 6 red, coverage read moved outside the tx = 1 red, moved before the lock = 1 red, `selectForUpdate` downgraded = 2 red, `billingIds[0]`-only = 1 red, **guard moved AFTER the insert = 5 red** (it still answers 409, so a status-only suite would have passed it — the "inserts NOTHING" assertions are what catch it). Not a tautology: with the guard gone every deny fixture reaches a real 201. **KNOWN CONSEQUENCE for demos:** the seed's 4 PVs cover 4 of the 6 `ap_billing` rows one-to-one, so a PV posted against a seeded covered billing now 409s where it used to 201 — `tests/e2e/finance-flow.spec.ts` mints a fresh billing at all 6 of its POST sites, so no current suite regresses. **Residuals, stated not buried:** (1) application-side only — no unique index makes double coverage impossible at the DB level, and the guard depends on READ COMMITTED (raising the isolation level silently disables it; the comment says so). (2) **raised by gate-4.5 and left for Wei rather than guessed:** because no cancelled/void PV state exists, the guard holds a billing hostage for the LIFE of any PV — including one raised in error, or one already exported to the bank. `ap.ts` marks the exact line a future cancel path must exclude. Today the only escape is that the erroneous PV keeps the billing, which fails closed (nothing is paid twice) but leaves the billing unpayable. 🟡 needs a ruling when a void/cancel flow is designed. |
| B-399 | api `bank` — batch_id has no semantics, no batch ENTITY is persisted, and the spec offers no re-export path | Follow-up filed by B-397 rather than guessed. (1) `batch_id` is defined only as "batch_id (Export to Bank)" (`data-dictionary.html:90`, `erd.html:85`) — no format, no enum, no lifecycle, and `docs/extract/MOCK-DATA.md:189` records it is absent from both mock sets. B-397 stamps a uuid; nothing says that is right. (2) The prototype's "ประวัติ Export" card (`bank.jsx:230-248`: file name · count · value · actor+date) has **no wire source** because only the stamp is persisted, not a batch row — the web still renders an honest empty state. (3) The spec has **no re-export affordance** at all: the only repeat action is re-downloading the same file from that history card, so under B-397's one-way lock a lost or bank-rejected file has no remedy but issuing a new voucher. (4) The two state labels the spec uses, "รอส่งธนาคาร" / "ส่งแล้ว" (`bank.jsx:166`), have **no key in i18n-full.json** — they are currently server-side reasoning only and cannot reach a screen without a sacred i18n round (PLAN.md §0 กฎข้อ 2). | (ก) persist a `bank_export_batch` entity (file name, count, total, actor, created_at) + FK from pv, which fills the history card and gives batch_id a meaning · (ข) keep the bare stamp and leave the history card empty forever · (ค) (ก) plus a re-DOWNLOAD op for an existing batch (matches the prototype's download icon) without ever re-emitting a payment instruction · พ่วง: mint the two filter labels so the sent/waiting split can appear on screen | — | 🟡 OPEN — รอ Wei · ไม่ block (ships fail-closed today: a sent voucher simply cannot be exported again) |

| B-401 | infra+api — **the runbook's own deploy command returned exit 1 on a completely correct deploy**, in BOTH the dev and prod compose | Found by the same live preflight: `docker compose -f infra/docker-compose.yml up -d --build --wait` (runbook §1 step 3) exited **1** with 5 of 6 services Healthy and `worker` permanently `Up (unhealthy)`. **Two stacked defects.** (a) `worker.ts` built its BullMQ connection from `REDIS_HOST`/`REDIS_PORT`, which are set nowhere as the worker's env, while both compose files supply `REDIS_URL` that the code never read — live log showed `connect ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:6379` in a permanent retry loop from the first second. (b) the worker inherited the API image's baked HTTP `/health` HEALTHCHECK (`apps/api/Dockerfile`) while serving no HTTP, so `--wait` — which waits for HEALTHY, not running — could never succeed. **Never caught locally because the local `juneflow-api:dev` image predated the Dockerfile gaining that HEALTHCHECK; a fresh `--build` on a host is the first build that bakes it in.** Blast radius is wider than staging: `.github/workflows/ci.yml:215` runs the same `up -d --wait` with no `continue-on-error`, so this was breaking a CI stage for every zone. | (ก) **[shipped]** parse `REDIS_URL` properly (host · port · ACL user · percent-decoded password · db index · TLS for `rediss:`) keeping REDIS_HOST/REDIS_PORT then localhost as fallback, throwing at boot on an unparseable URL rather than falling back silently (the `resolveAuthSecret` precedent — silent fallback is exactly what hid this for a whole deploy cycle); and give `worker` its own healthcheck in BOTH compose files · (ข) `healthcheck: disable: true` | **ก** (2026-08-14 · orch-B — infra defect, no design ruling needed) | ✅ RESOLVED — merged. **The implementer refused the (ข) shortcut this row offered, and was right:** during the live failure `unhealthy` was the only TRUE statement the stack was making — only the probe was wrong — so disabling would have reported 6/6 Healthy while the worker looped on ECONNREFUSED, deleting the one signal that caught the bug. A `pgrep` probe and a "can I reach redis:6379" probe were rejected for the sharper reason that **both are green in exactly the failure they must catch** (process alive, Redis reachable, worker dialling the wrong host). The shipped probe reads a heartbeat file `worker.ts` refreshes ONLY while every queue's connection reports `status === "ready"`. **Proven LIVE after the fix: `up -d --build --wait` EXIT=0 in 177s, all six services Healthy including worker, heartbeat age 394ms against a 20s threshold.** The same defect existed in `infra/docker-compose.prod.yml`, whose worker had no healthcheck either — and `infra/runbook.md:56` is the prod `up -d --build --wait`, so the PROD deploy command was equally broken; the same block was mirrored there and `config` renders clean. **Test-gap closed on a verifier's finding:** the heartbeat itself was initially unprotected (both a "write unconditionally" and a "delete the block" mutation left the suite green 10/10), so `worker.test.ts` grew to 16 tests and both mutations now go red. **Residual:** the db-index path segment is unvalidated (`redis://h:6379/abc` → `db: NaN`); unreachable under either compose, noted not chased. |

เช็ค `rest.length === 0` แต่ pino เช็ค `msg === undefined` ต่างกันหนึ่ง argument · (4) provenance เช็ค `typeof code === "string"` ซึ่งไม่ใช่ SQLSTATE ทำให้ `ECONNREFUSED` ถูก log ว่า "Postgres refused this statement (SQLSTATE ECONNREFUSED)" — **เป็นเท็จและลบ host:port ทิ้ง** ทั้งที่ B-033 วินิจฉัยจากสตริงนั้น · (5) cause echo ที่เพิ่มเพื่อกู้ address **เป็น denylist และค่าพารามิเตอร์จริงหลุดผ่าน** (`circular reference detected while preparing "<marker>" for query` ลงดิสก์) และ fail-safe rule ที่เพิ่มมา**ทำลายข้อความของ error ธรรมดา** เพราะรับฟิลด์ชื่อ `severity` ซึ่งเป็น**คอลัมน์จริงใน schema นี้** (`subcon.ts:189`). **ตัดสินใจเชิงออกแบบ: หยุดเพิ่มชั้น ถอดสองชั้นหลังทิ้ง** — heuristic ทับ heuristic เหนือสตริงที่เชื่อไม่ได้ แลก diagnosability กับ leak สลับกันทุกรอบ. เหลือแกนที่ mutate แล้วตายทุกจุด: allowlist · hook · `rest[0] === undefined` · `isPostgresError` (SQLSTATE 5 ตัว **และ** `severity` — วัดกับ error จริง 8 คลาสจาก PG 16) · `detail` เฉพาะ class 22 (23505 detail คือ `Key (vendor_tax_id)=(<ค่า>) already exists.` ซึ่ง B-165/B-167 ทำให้เป็นเรื่องปกติ). **ต้นทุนที่ยอมรับและบันทึกไว้:** connection failure เหลือ type + stack frames — ไม่มีทั้ง address และ code (host แบบ dual-stack เช่น `localhost` ซึ่งเป็นค่า default ของ repo ให้ `AggregateError` ที่ `message` ว่างและ `.errors` ไม่ใช่ own-enumerable) **ทางแก้ที่ถูกคือ log host จาก config ของแอปเอง ไม่ใช่แกะจากสตริง error** — เขียนไว้ใน header แล้วว่าทำไมถึงเลิกแกะ. api **1871 → 1892** |

| B-405 | infra — guard ของ staging ถูกปลอมได้ด้วย env var ที่ runbook สอนให้ตั้งเอง (ปิดแล้ว บันทึกไว้เป็นคลาส) | บันทึกเพราะ guard นี้ถูกเขียนผิด **3 รูปแบบติดกัน** และทุกครั้งเป็น "ประมาณเงื่อนไขจริง" ไม่ใช่ "ตรงกับมัน": (1) `grep -c 'db seed'` — **exit code กลับด้าน** (สถานะปลอดภัยพิมพ์ 0 exit **1** · สถานะล้างฐานพิมพ์ 1 exit **0**) และ fail-open (เปลี่ยน base เป็น `pnpm --filter @juneflow/db run seed` ความหมายเท่าเดิม → guard บอก "ปลอดภัย") · (2) `grep -cE 'host_ip: 127.0.0.1|db migrate$' \| grep -qx 3` — **นับบรรทัดจาก union pattern** ไม่ตรวจว่า loopback เป็นของ service ไหน วัดจริงโดยไม่แก้ไฟล์: `POSTGRES_PORT=127.0.0.1:5432 REDIS_PORT=... API_PORT=...` (สามตัวที่ runbook ข้อ 2 บอกให้ตั้ง) → พิมพ์ `3` exit 0 บน config ที่ command จริงคือ `migrate && seed` · (3) `config migrate-seed \| grep -qxE ' *- pnpm …migrate'` — **YAML render ปลอมได้**: `config migrate-seed` render service ที่มัน `depends_on` ออกมาด้วย (postgres) และ compose แปลงค่าที่มี newline เป็น block scalar ดังนั้น `POSTGRES_PASSWORD=$'x\n- pnpm --filter @juneflow/db migrate'` **งอกเป็นบรรทัดที่ตรง pattern** → ACCEPT บน config ที่จะ TRUNCATE | (ก) **[shipped]** อ่านผ่าน `--format json` (JSON escape `\n` ค่า interpolate จึงสร้างบรรทัดไม่ได้) + นับ entry ต่อ service ด้วย `awk` `p==1 && l==1` แทนการหา "มี loopback สักบรรทัด" | **ก** (2026-08-15 · orch-B) | ✅ RESOLVED — merged. ทดสอบ **9 สถานะโดยดึงคำสั่งออกมาจาก `runbook.md` เอง** (ไม่พิมพ์ใหม่ = ทดสอบสิ่งที่ ship): 2 ACCEPT (overlay ON จาก repo root และจาก `$HOME`) · 7 REFUSE (base only · `COMPOSE_FILE` ไม่ตั้ง · `cd infra` · base+3 loopback vars · base+newline password จากสอง cwd · ขาด secret). **บทเรียนของแถวนี้คือรูปแบบ ไม่ใช่บั๊ก** — guard ที่ประมาณเงื่อนไขจริงจะเขียวในสถานะที่มันมีไว้กันพอดี · และ**คำกล่าวแบบสัมบูรณ์ 5 ข้อในงานชุดนี้ถูกการวัดหักล้าง** ("ปลอมไม่ได้" · "ไม่มีอะไรสำคัญหาย" · "seed idempotent ข้อมูลอยู่ครบ" · "ไม่มี env var ไหนทำได้" · "พิสูจน์ว่าไม่มี publish นอก loopback") ทุกข้อถูกแทนด้วยขอบเขตที่วัดได้แล้ว |

ดังนั้นการเติมแค่ browser จะทำให้ทุกจอ skip แล้ว stage เขียวโดยไม่ถ่ายอะไรเลย — gate ปลอมคลาส B-384 และในกรณีนี้แย่กว่าแดง เพราะ PLAN.md §0 กฎข้อ 4 ให้ visual gate เป็นเกณฑ์ตัดสิน design fidelity · **สิ่งที่ยังพิสูจน์ไม่ได้ตอนนี้และต้องอ่านจาก run ถัดไป:** จอ 100+ จอตรง reference จริงหรือไม่ — คำถามนี้ยังไม่เคยมีใครตอบได้เลยตั้งแต่ตั้ง CI มา เพราะ stage นี้ไม่เคยรัน |

05:22 UTC` ตรงเป๊ะ):** seed สด → **14 จอ** · **seed ตรึง → 9 จอ** และเซต 9 จอนี้ **เท่ากันทุกตัวกับที่วัดได้เมื่อหลายชั่วโมงก่อน** จึงนิ่ง ไม่ขึ้นกับเวลาแล้ว · **9 จอที่เหลือมีคำอธิบายครบทุกจอ จึงตัดเรื่องสี/ระยะ/ข้อความออกได้ · **สมมติฐาน 'กลุ่ม 3 คือนาฬิกาของแอป' ที่ผมเสนอไว้ ถูกหักล้าง**: ตรึงนาฬิกาเบราว์เซอร์ด้วยแล้วสี่จอนั้นยังตกเหมือนเดิม · **ข้อสรุปสำหรับการลงมือ:** กลุ่ม 1 **ยืนยันซ้ำ:** คอนฟิก seed-ตรึงถูกวัดทั้งหมด **5 รอบ** ได้เซต 9 จอเดิมเป๊ะ **4 รอบ** และ 10 จอ 1 รอบ — ตัวที่เกินคือ `master-cc` ซึ่งจึง**แกว่งเอง 1 ใน 5** ไม่ใช่ผลของนาฬิกา (จอนี้ไม่มี mask เลย ขณะที่ 72 ใน 99 จอมี) · **สมมติฐาน 'ตรึงนาฬิกาเบราว์เซอร์ช่วย' ถูกหักล้างด้วยการวัด** จึงถอดโค้ดนั้นออก ไม่ ship กลไกที่พิสูจน์ไม่ได้เข้า CI · **ผลต่อจำนวนที่ต้องถ่ายใหม่: 9 ใบ ไม่ใช่ 5 ใบตามที่รายงาน Wei ตอนขออนุมัติ** — หลักการที่อนุมัติ (ถ่ายเฉพาะที่เก่าจริง ไม่ทับทั้ง pack) ไม่เปลี่ยน แต่สมาชิกเปลี่ยนตามการวัดเพราะสี่จอที่เดาว่าเป็นนาฬิกาแอปกลายเป็นของเก่าจริง · **ด่านของการถ่าย:** ถ่ายทั้ง 99 (promote เป็น all-or-nothing โดยออกแบบ) แต่ต้องออกมาเปลี่ยนเฉพาะ 9 ไฟล์นี้เป๊ะ มิฉะนั้นคืน pack ทันที · และต้องถ่ายสองรอบบนสแต็กสดคนละรอบให้ manifest ตรงกัน ซึ่งเป็นด่านที่กัน `master-cc` ที่แกว่งไม่ให้ถูกบันทึกเป็น baseline · **📌 อ่านพิกเซลจริงแล้ว (17 ส.ค. · เก็บ diff จากสแต็กที่ตรึงนาฬิกา แล้วดูภาพเอง ไม่ใช่อนุมานจาก `git log` แบบรอบก่อน) — กลไกของสี่จอที่เคยเรียกว่า 'กลุ่ม 3' ชัดแล้วและไม่ใช่ทั้งนาฬิกาและไม่ใช่ UI:** `petty` — คอลัมน์วันที่ทุกแถวอ่าน `2026-08-07` แบบจาง (ตรงกับ baseline) แต่**แดงเฉพาะส่วนชั่วโมง:นาที** ทั้ง 6 แถว → `SEED_FROZEN_NOW` ทำให้วันที่ตรงได้ แต่ B-323 เปลี่ยนวิธีประทับเป็น `SEED_NOW − i·step` ส่วน baseline เก่าได้เวลาจาก `defaultNow()` ณ วินาทีที่ถ่าย — **คนละข้อมูล ไม่มีการตรึงนาฬิกาใดทำให้ตรงได้** · `notifications` — แดงเฉพาะป้ายวันที่/อายุสัมพัทธ์ ส่วนหัวเรื่องที่เป็น UUID ไม่แดงเลย → แถวเดียวกัน เวลาต่างกัน กลไกเดียวกับ petty · `gl-inbox` — เวลาต่างเหมือนกัน **บวกกับยอดเงิน `165,960` ของแถว GR ห้าแถวเป็นสีแดงขณะที่เลขที่เอกสาร `GR-2026-0148…0144` ไม่แดง** → เลขเอกสารเดิมแต่ค่าต่าง คือข้อมูลเปลี่ยน ไม่ใช่เวลาอย่างเดียว · `dashboard` — แดงที่ป้ายแกน X ของกราฟและแท่งท้าย ๆ โดยเอเจนต์ที่อ่านแบบปิดตารายงานว่า **เลื่อนไปข้างหน้าหนึ่งเดือนทั้งแกน** ซึ่งนาฬิกาเบราว์เซอร์อธิบายไม่ได้ (7→17 ส.ค. ไม่ข้ามเดือน) แต่ช่วงเดือนที่มาจากข้อมูลอธิบายได้ · **ข้อสรุปที่เปลี่ยนเหตุผล ไม่เปลี่ยนการกระทำ:** ทั้งสี่จอเป็น *ข้อมูลที่ประทับเวลา/ค่าใหม่ตามกติกา B-323* จึงถ่าย baseline ใหม่คือทางแก้ที่ถูก **และคราวนี้จะนิ่งจริง** เพราะ stamp ใหม่กำหนดได้แล้วเมื่อตรึง `SEED_FROZEN_NOW` (ต่างจากถ่ายใหม่ตอนไม่ตรึง ซึ่งจะเก่าอีกครั้งวันรุ่งขึ้น) · การอ่านนี้ใช้ **จอควบคุมสองใบ**: `gl-coa` ที่ผ่าน gate (0 พิกเซลต่าง) และ `pm-wo` (5,292 พิกเซล ต่างมากสุด) · **และจอควบคุมใบหลังหักล้างคำอธิบายของผมเอง:** ผมเคยจัด `pm-*` ว่า "UI เปลี่ยนจริง" เพราะ `screens/pm` มี 8 commit แต่พิกเซลบอกคนละเรื่อง — `pm-wo` แดงที่**ชื่อ/รหัสอุปกรณ์และชื่อช่างในแถว 2–6 (ข้อความสองชุดซ้อนกัน) ขณะที่แถวแรกตรงกัน** คือ **ลำดับ/ชุดแถวเปลี่ยน ไม่ใช่เลย์เอาต์** และ B-323 ก็แก้ `apps/api/src/routes/wo.ts` ไว้ในกลุ่มบังคับลำดับด้วย · **ผลคือสาเหตุเป็นเนื้อเดียวกันกว่าที่ผมเคยเขียน: ทุกจอที่ตรวจแล้วต่างเพราะข้อมูล/ลำดับที่ B-323 เปลี่ยน ไม่ใช่เพราะ UI** — ซึ่งไม่เปลี่ยนการกระทำ (ยังต้องถ่ายใหม่) แต่เปลี่ยนเหตุผลที่จะเขียนลง commit และเปลี่ยนสิ่งที่ควรคาดหวังหลังถ่าย · **ลำดับก่อนหลังที่ต้องเคารพ:** การถ่ายใหม่ต้องเกิด **หลัง** ชุด B-411 ถูก commit (เพื่อให้ถ่ายด้วย harness ตัวสุดท้าย) และ **คำตอบของ B-412 เป็นเงื่อนไขโดยตรง** — ถ้า seed ไม่นิ่งข้ามสแต็ก pack ใหม่จะตกอีกในสแต็กถัดไป คือปัญหา B-323 ซ้ำรอย · ข้อดีคือ**ด่านนี้บังคับตัวเองอยู่แล้ว**: สคริปต์ถ่ายต้องได้ manifest เท่ากันจากสองสแต็กสด ถ้า seed ไม่นิ่งมันจะต่างและสคริปต์จะคืน pack แล้วปฏิเสธเอง ไม่ต้องพึ่งวินัยของคนถ่าย · **🔴 การแก้ครั้งใหญ่ที่สุดของแถวนี้ และเป็นการแก้ตัวผมเอง (17 ส.ค. ดึก):** `tests/visual/screens.manifest.json` บันทึก provenance ของ pack ไว้เองว่า *"FULL RE-BASELINE (2026-08-07 · orch-B QA) … with `SEED_FROZEN_NOW=2026-07-20T09:00:00Z` (B-224 — freezes the seed clock AND the ap/ar aging clock) … then proven strict-0"* → **pack ถูกถ่ายด้วยนาฬิกาที่ตรึงไว้แล้ว เพียงแต่เป็นคนละ instant** ส่วนค่าที่ผมใส่ไว้ก่อนหน้า (`2026-08-07T12:22:42+07:00` = เวลาคอมมิตของ `91dee5c`) เป็นการ**เดาจากคอมมิต ไม่ใช่จากหลักฐาน** — แก้ใน `ci.yml` และ README แล้ว · **ข้อสรุปเดิมของผมที่ผิดไปด้วย:** ที่เขียนว่า "baseline เก่าได้เวลาจาก `defaultNow()` ณ วินาทีที่ถ่าย" ไม่จริง มันถูกตรึงไว้แล้ว · 09:00`) ได้ **15 จอตก ไม่ใช่ 9** — `dashboard` หลุดจากรายการ (ยืนยันว่าแกนเดือนเป็นเรื่อง instant จริง) แต่มีจอที่ขึ้นกับอายุ/วันที่ โผล่มาแทนเจ็ดจอ (`ar-invoice` · `bank-recon` · `po-list` · `boq-editor` · `boq-overview` · `gr-list` · `dms`) · **ข้อสรุปที่แข็งกว่าเดิม: ไม่มี instant ใดทำให้ pack กลับมาตรง เพราะ B-323 เปลี่ยน *อัลกอริทึม* การประทับ ไม่ใช่แค่เลื่อนเวลา → การถ่ายใหม่จำเป็นจริง และค่าที่ควรใช้คือค่าที่รีโปเลือกและมีเอกสารรองรับ ไม่ใช่ค่าที่ทำให้ gate ใจดีกว่า** · **คำอธิบาย pm-* ของผมถูกหักล้างเป็นรอบที่สอง อย่างละเอียดกว่าเดิม:** ไฟล์ที่วาดจอ `pm-wo` คือ `wo-list.tsx` และ `wo-rows.ts` **เหมือน baseline ทุกไบต์** คอมมิตทั้ง 8 ตัวใน `screens/pm` เป็นงาน signature pad ของจอ *detail* คนละจอ · สาเหตุจริงคือ `pm.ts:583 → 585` ที่ได้ `newestFirst()` | B-410 | web+infra — **image ของ `web` ยิง API ผิด path ทั้งใบตั้งแต่ 10 ส.ค.: ทุกจอเรนเดอร์เปลือกเปล่า ไม่มีข้อมูล ไม่มี error** | **เจอเพราะ guard ของ B-409 ปฏิเสธ ไม่ใช่เพราะมีใครสังเกต** — ตอนถ่าย baseline ใหม่ B-155 near-duplicate detector refuse การ promote: 5 จอ (`master-cc · master-docnum · master-model · master-ptype · users`) ห่างกันเพียง 0.2953–0.6648 ทั้งที่ใน pack ที่อนุมัติแล้วห้าจอเดียวกันห่างกัน **3.4246–6.2388** (ยุบลง ~10 เท่า) → **ไม่มีไฟล์ใดถูกเขียน** และ pack ยืนยันว่าไม่ถูกแตะ. **ต้นเหตุ:** `apps/web/Dockerfile` เขียน `ARG VITE_API_BASE_URL` แล้วตามด้วย `ENV VITE_API_BASE_URL=${VITE_API_BASE_URL}` — Docker แทน ARG ที่ไม่ถูกส่งด้วย**สตริงว่าง** แล้ว `ENV` **นิยาม**ตัวแปรนั้น ไม่ใช่ปล่อยว่าง · Vite จึง inline `""` และ `api-client.ts:23` `import.meta.env.VITE_API_BASE_URL ?? "/api/v1"` **ไม่ fallback** เพราะ `??` จับเฉพาะ null/undefined → `API_BASE_URL = ""` ทุก request หลุด prefix ไปที่ root. คอมเมนต์ในไฟล์ที่อ้างว่า *"Left unset on purpose: api-client.ts then falls back to the same-origin /api/v1"* **เป็นเท็จ**. **หลักฐานสองทางอิสระ:** (1) *สถิต* — bundle ใน image ที่ build ไม่ส่ง arg มีสตริง `/api/v1` **0 ครั้ง** และมี `"/counts"` อยู่ (2) *พลวัต* — เบราว์เซอร์บนสแต็ก compose ยิง `http://127.0.0.1:PORT/counts?keys=…` ที่ root ส่วน `nginx.conf.template:65` proxy เฉพาะ `location /api/` → path ที่ root ตกไป SPA fallback ได้ **index.html 200** จึงไม่มี console error สักบรรทัดและไม่มีข้อมูลสักแถว. **ทำไมกลายเป็นจอว่างทั้งระบบ:** `use-shell-data.ts:126,146` auto-select `projects[0]`/`companies[0]` — ลิสต์ว่างจึงไม่มีบริษัท/โครงการถูกเลือก แล้ว query ที่ scope ตามโครงการคืนศูนย์แถวทั้งแอป (API เองปกติดี: ถามตรง ๆ ได้ **companies=3 · projects=7**). **คอมมิตต้นทาง `aba803f` (2026-08-10)** ชื่อคอมมิตคือ *"fix(web,api): the web image could not reach the API at all, and answered 200 while failing"* — เติม nginx proxy ให้ `/api/v1` ถูกแล้วครึ่งหนึ่ง แต่ทำ bundle เลิกยิง `/api/v1` ในคอมมิตเดียวกัน. **ทำไมไม่มีเกตไหนจับได้ 7 วัน:** vitest รันบนซอร์สที่ตัวแปร undefined จริง → `/api/v1` เขียว · contract/E2E เป็นระดับ API (37 ใน 40 ไฟล์ · 25 อ้างอิง `:3000` · `page.goto` เพียง 3 จุด) · เทสต์ล็อกอินผ่านเบราว์เซอร์ตัวเดียวที่จะจับได้คือ `smoke.spec.ts:88` ซึ่งอยู่ใต้ `liveDescribe` ที่ `smoke.spec.ts:57-58` ผูกกับ `E2E_LIVE` และ **`E2E_LIVE` ไม่เคยปรากฏใน `ci.yml` เลย** → skip เสมอ · เหลือ visual gate เป็นสิ่งเดียวที่เรนเดอร์ bundle จริงในเบราว์เซอร์ และมันไม่เคยรัน (B-401 → B-407 → B-408) — **บั๊กลงวันที่ 10 ส.ค. พอดีกับช่วงที่เกตเดียวที่มองเห็นมันตายอยู่** | (ก) **[ทำแล้ว]** แก้สองชั้นอิสระ: `Dockerfile` ระบุ default ตรง ๆ `ARG VITE_API_BASE_URL=/api/v1` (ไม่พึ่ง fallback ปลายทาง) **และ** `api-client.ts` เพิ่ม `resolveApiBaseUrl()` ที่ถือค่าว่าง/ช่องว่างล้วนเป็น "ไม่ได้ตั้ง" · (ข) แก้เฉพาะ Dockerfile — ปฏิเสธ: เป็นครั้งที่สามที่จอนี้เป็นตัวแยกของอาการตระกูลนี้. หลักฐานประกอบ:** รัน gate ในเครื่องโดยส่ง storageState ที่ล็อกอินเป็น `wipha@rungrueang.co.th` → **ตก 11 จอ** · CI run `32016968465` ที่คอมมิต `70ea1e2` (มี B-410 fix ครบ) → **ตก 99 จอ** และ `passed` ขึ้นจาก 16 เป็น 79 ซึ่งพอดีกับ self-test 63 ตัวที่คอมมิตนั้นเพิ่ง เพิ่ม คือ**จำนวนจอที่ตกไม่ขยับเลยสักจอ** · diffRatio ใน CI = **0.014–0.75** ซึ่งเป็นลายเซ็นของจอเปล่า ไม่ใช่ drift เล็ก (ของจริงหลังแก้คือ 0.000155–0.011). **สาเหตุ อ่านจากไฟล์ตรง ๆ:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` Stage 6 มีเพียง `Set up pnpm` → `Set up Node` → `Install dependencies` → `Start dev stack (docker compose)` → `Install Playwright browsers` → `pnpm run --if-present test:visual` → `Stop dev stack` — **ไม่มีขั้นตอน login และไม่ตั้ง `VISUAL_STORAGE_STATE`** · `tests/package.json` `test:visual` = `playwright test --config visual/playwright.visual.config.ts` เฉย ๆ · root `test:visual` = `pnpm --dir tests run test:visual` · `grep VISUAL_STORAGE_STATE package.json tests/package.json .github/workflows/ci.yml` → **ไม่พบเลยสักที่** · `tests/visual/playwright.visual.config.ts:49` คือ `storageState: process.env.VISUAL_STORAGE_STATE \|\| undefined` จึงเป็น `undefined` ใน CI เสมอ · ส่วน baseline ที่อนุมัติไว้ถ่ายแบบล็อกอิน — เห็นได้จากภาพเองที่มีตัวเลือกบริษัท/โครงการและชิปผู้ใช้ "วิภา จันทร์เจริญ · Director · CONS" มุมล่าง และจาก `visual-gate.spec.ts:374` ที่ระบุว่า owner token เรนเดอร์ทั้งสอง sidebar. **ผลที่ตามมาซึ่งใหญ่กว่าตัวเลข:** ตั้งแต่ Stage 6 เริ่มรันได้จริง (B-408) มันไม่เคยวัด design fidelity เลย — มันวัด "จอที่ล็อกเอาต์ ไม่ตรงกับจอที่ล็อกอิน" ซึ่งจริงเสมอ | (ก) **[แนะนำ]** เติมขั้นตอน login ให้ Stage 6 แล้วส่ง `VISUAL_STORAGE_STATE`: หลัง `Start dev stack` ยิง `POST /api/v1/auth/login` ด้วย seed user แล้วเขียนไฟล์ storageState (origin = baseURL, key `juneflow-token`) — ทำแบบเดียวกับที่ `agents/orch-b-recon/phase4-g5-rebaseline.sh` และสคริปต์ re-baseline ใช้อยู่แล้ว · **ไม่ต้องใช้ secret ใด ๆ**: ผู้ใช้อยู่ใน seed จริง (`packages/db/src/seed/index.ts:209`) และรหัสผ่านเป็นข้อมูล dev ที่สคริปต์ในรีโปใช้เป็นข้อความธรรมดาอยู่แล้ว จึงเป็นแค่ curl หนึ่งขั้นตอน · เป็น sacred edit `.github/workflows/ci.yml` ต้องมี `SACRED_OVERRIDE=wei-approved:B-411` · (ข) **ควรทำคู่กัน ไม่ใช่แทน** (แก้ `tests/` ไม่ใช่ sacred): ให้ gate **fail ดัง ๆ** เมื่อไม่มี session แทนที่จะถ่ายจอล็อกเอาต์เงียบ ๆ — ตอนนี้ `VISUAL_STORAGE_STATE` ที่หายไปทำให้ "ไม่ได้ล็อกอิน" ปลอมตัวเป็น "99 จอ drift" ได้ ซึ่งเป็นคลาสเดียวกับ gate ปลอมใน B-384/B-408 · (ค) ถ่าย baseline ใหม่แบบไม่ล็อกอิน — **ปฏิเสธ**: นี่คือ ERP ที่ทุกจอต้องมี session · pack จะกลายเป็นหน้า login 99 ใบ และ gate จะไม่วัดอะไรเลย | **ก** (2026-08-17 · Wei — อนุมัติทั้งสองส่วน: sacred edit `.github/workflows/ci.yml` เติมขั้นตอน login + ส่ง `VISUAL_STORAGE_STATE` **และ** แก้ `tests/` ให้ gate fail ดัง ๆ เมื่อไม่มี session) | ✅ RESOLVED — merged ด้วย `SACRED_OVERRIDE=wei-approved:B-411` ตามคำตัดสิน ก (ทั้งสองส่วน) · **ส่วน sacred:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml` Stage 6 ได้ step `Mint the visual-gate session` (login → เขียน storageState → export `VISUAL_STORAGE_STATE` · **ล้มทันทีถ้าไม่ได้ token**) และ `SEED_FROZEN_NOW` บน step ยกสแต็ก (B-409 กลุ่ม 1) — diff **เพิ่มล้วน — `git diff --numstat` ฝั่งลบเป็น 0** (ตัวเลขฝั่งเพิ่มไม่ถูกอ้างไว้ตรงนี้โดยตั้งใจ เพราะมันขยับทุกครั้งที่แก้คอมเมนต์ และเคยผิดมาแล้วหนึ่งรอบ) · **ส่วน `tests/`:** การตรวจ session ทำงานทั้งสองโหมด และการตัดสินใจถูกย้ายออกมาเป็น `resolveGateSessionDecision` ใน `lib/promote.ts` เพื่อให้ unit-test ได้ (beforeAll เป็นตะเข็บที่เทสต์เข้าไม่ถึงถ้าไม่มีสแต็กจริง) · **ปิดทางเขียวเงียบที่ตัวเองเพิ่งเปิด:** `test.skip(!reachable)` แปลว่าแอปไม่ถึง = ข้ามทั้ง 99 จอ = exit 0 ดังนั้นพอ `appReachable` เข้มขึ้นแล้ว การเปลี่ยน `<title>`/`id="root"` จะทำให้ stage เขียวโดยไม่เทียบอะไรเลย — รอบที่ **ตั้งใจจะเทียบ** (promote เปิด หรือมี session) จึงถูก **ปฏิเสธ** ไม่ใช่ข้าม — **ขอบเขตที่ซื่อสัตย์: ปิดเฉพาะการตรวจครั้งแรกใน `beforeAll`** ส่วน `test.skip(!(await appReachable(...)))` รายจอยังประเมินซ้ำอีก 99 ครั้งด้วย timeout 1500 ms และยัง *ข้าม* เงียบ ๆ ได้รายจอ → ยื่นเป็น B-414 · **acceptance บนสแต็กจริง:** ไม่มี session → `1 failed · 98 did not run` พร้อมบอกสาเหตุ · มี session → เดินหน้าเทียบจริง · self-test **132 ผ่าน** (ตัวเลขนี้ขยับทุกครั้งที่เพิ่มเทสต์ · สิ่งที่คงทนคือ **0 ตก**) · gate 4.5 **FAIL เจ็ดรอบ** ก่อนผ่าน — สามรอบแรกวัดได้ว่าการเปลี่ยนแปลงหลัก**ยังไม่ตายเมื่อย้อนกลับ** (รอบสุดท้ายพบว่าผมย้าย routing ลง `lib/` แล้วทิ้งสำเนาไว้ในสเปก จึงต้องเพิ่ม enforce test ที่อ่านซอร์สของสเปกเอง) และรอบที่สี่จับได้ว่า **origin ของ storageState ไม่เคยถูกตรวจกับ `baseURL`** ซึ่งทำให้ B-411 กลับมาได้เงียบ ๆ ทั้งที่ guard เขียวหมด — แก้แล้วทั้งสองข้อ |

เกิดระหว่างเซสชัน ไม่ใช่ของเดิม: ต้นเซสชัน `npx playwright --version` ในเขต `tests` ตอบ **1.61.1** (ตรวจตอนดูว่ามี `page.clock` ไหม) จากนั้นกลางทาง capture รอบหนึ่งตายด้วย `Playwright Test did not expect test.describe() to be called here` โดย stack trace ชี้ไปที่ `tests/node_modules/.pnpm/playwright@1.62.1/...` — ซึ่งเป็นสาเหตุที่ error นั้นบอกไว้เองเป็นข้อแรกว่า *"You have two different
